The sum is equal to

[formula]

\frac{1}{2\pi i}\int_{(1)}\bigg(\sum_{n}\frac{\chi(n)\xi_{0j}(n;d,r)}{n^{1+s}}\bigg)\frac{x^{s}\,ds}{(s+\beta_{\mu})^{2}}.

The contour of integration is moved in the same way as in the proof of Lemma 8.2. Recall that

\sum_{n}\frac{\chi(n)\xi_{0j}(n;d,r)}{n^{1+s}}=\frac{L(1+s+\beta_{j+1},\chi)L(1+s+\beta_{j+2},\chi)}{L(1+s,\chi)}\mathcal{U}_{j}(d,r,1+s).

By Lemma 5.5 and 5.6, for
|s|=5\alpha
we have

\frac{L(1+s+\beta_{j+1},\chi)L(1+s+\beta_{j+2},\chi)}{L(1+s,\chi)}=L^{\prime}(1,\chi)\frac{(s+\beta_{j+1})(s+\beta_{j+2})}{s}+O(\mathcal{L}^{-15}).

Combining these results with Lemma 8.3, we find that the integral (8.9) is equal to

L^{\prime}(1,\chi)\Pi(d,r)\cdot\frac{1}{2\pi i}\int_{|s|=5\alpha}\frac{(s+\beta_{j+1})(s+\beta_{j+2})}{s}\frac{x^{s}\,ds}{(s+\beta_{\mu})^{2}}+O(\mathcal{L}^{-6}).

The result now follows by direct calculation.
\Box

By Lemma 8.2 with
x=P_{1}/dr
and
x=P_{2}/dr
respectively we have

\sum_{m}\frac{\chi(m)\varkappa_{1}(drm)}{m^{1-\beta_{j}}}=\frac{L^{\prime}(1,\chi)}{\log P_{1}}\mathcal{F}_{j6}(P_{1}/dr)+O(\mathcal{L}^{-15})

if
dr<P_{1}/T
, and

\sum_{m}\frac{\chi(m)\varkappa_{2}(drm)}{m^{1-\beta_{j}}}=\frac{L^{\prime}(1,\chi)}{\log P_{2}}\mathcal{F}_{j7}(P_{2}/dr)+O(\mathcal{L}^{-15})

if
dr<P_{2}/T
. On the other hand, by Lemma 8.3 with
x=P_{1}/dr
and
x=P_{2}/dr
respectively we have

\sum_{n}\frac{\chi(n)\bar{\varkappa}_{1}(drn)\xi_{0j}(n;d,r)}{n}=\frac{L^{\prime}(1,\chi)\Pi(d,r)}{\log P_{1}}\mathcal{G}_{j6}(P_{1}/dr)+O(\mathcal{L}^{-15})

if
dr<P_{1}/T
, and

\sum_{n}\frac{\chi(n)\bar{\varkappa}_{2}(drn)\xi_{0j}(n;d,r)}{n}=\frac{L^{\prime}(1,\chi)\Pi(d,r)}{\log P_{2}}\mathcal{G}_{j7}(P_{2}/dr)+O(\mathcal{L}^{-15})

if
dr<P_{2}/T
. Gathering these results together we conclude, by simple approximation, that

\displaystyle S_{j}(\mathbb{a}_{11},\mathbb{a}_{21})=

\displaystyle L^{\prime}(1,\chi)^{2}\sum_{dr<P_{2}}\frac{|\mu(r)\chi(dr)|}{dr\varphi(r)}\lambda_{0j}(dr)\Pi(d,r)

\displaystyle\times\bigg(\frac{\mathcal{F}_{j6}(P_{1}/dr)}{\log P_{1}}+\iota_{2}\frac{\mathcal{F}_{j7}(P_{2}/dr)}{\log P_{2}}\bigg)\bigg(\frac{\mathcal{G}_{j6}(P_{1}/dr)}{\log P_{1}}+\bar{\iota}_{2}\frac{\mathcal{G}_{j7}(P_{2}/dr)}{\log P_{2}}\bigg)

\displaystyle+L^{\prime}(1,\chi)^{2}\sum_{P_{2}\leq dr<P_{1}}\frac{|\mu(r)\chi(dr)|}{dr\varphi(r)}\lambda_{0j}(dr)\Pi(d,r)\frac{\mathcal{F}_{j6}(P_{1}/dr)\mathcal{G}_{j6}(P_{1}/dr)}{(\log P_{1})^{2}}+o(\alpha).

It can be shown, by verifying the case
n=q^{k}
, that

\sum_{n=dr}\frac{|\mu(r)|}{r}\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}(q,r)=1\\
q|d\end{subarray}}(1-q^{-1}-\chi(q)q^{-1})=\prod_{q|n}(1-\chi(q)q^{-1}),

so that

[formula]

\sum_{n=dr}\frac{|\mu(r)|}{\varphi(r)}\Pi(d,r)=\frac{n}{\varphi(n)}.

It follows, by substituting
n=dr
, that

\displaystyle S_{j}(\mathbb{a}_{11},\mathbb{a}_{21})=

\displaystyle L^{\prime}(1,\chi)^{2}\sum_{n<P_{2}}\frac{|\chi(n)|\lambda_{0j}(n)}{\varphi(n)}

\displaystyle\times\bigg(\frac{\mathcal{F}_{j6}(P_{1}/n)}{\log P_{1}}+\iota_{2}\frac{\mathcal{F}_{j7}(P_{2}/n)}{\log P_{2}}\bigg)\bigg(\frac{\mathcal{G}_{j6}(P_{1}/n)}{\log P_{1}}+\bar{\iota}_{2}\frac{\mathcal{G}_{j7}(P_{2}/n)}{\log P_{2}}\bigg)

\displaystyle+L^{\prime}(1,\chi)^{2}\sum_{P_{2}\leq n<P_{1}}\frac{|\chi(n)|\lambda_{0j}(n)}{\varphi(n)}\frac{\mathcal{F}_{j6}(P_{1}/n)\mathcal{G}_{j6}(P_{1}/n)}{(\log P_{1})^{2}}+o(\alpha).

Since for
n<P
,

\lambda_{0j}(n)=\prod_{q|n}(1-q^{-1})^{2}(1+O(\alpha\log q/q))=\frac{\varphi(n)^{2}}{n^{2}}+O(\alpha_{1})

and, for
x<P
,

\sum_{n<x}\frac{|\chi(n)|\varphi(n)}{n^{2}}=\frac{\varphi(D)}{D}\bigg(\prod_{(q,D)=1}(1-q^{-2})\bigg)\log x+O(\log\mathcal{L})=\frac{6}{\pi^{2}}\bigg(\prod_{q|D}\frac{q}{q+1}\bigg)\log x+O(\log\mathcal{L}),

(see [T, 1.2.12]), it follows by partial integration that

[formula]

\displaystyle S_{j}(\mathbb{a}_{11},\mathbb{a}_{21})=

\displaystyle\mathfrak{a}\int_{1}^{P_{2}}\bigg(\frac{\mathcal{F}_{j6}(P_{1}/x)}{\log P_{1}}+\iota_{2}\frac{\mathcal{F}_{j7}(P_{2}/x)}{\log P_{2}}\bigg)\bigg(\frac{\mathcal{G}_{j6}(P_{1}/x)}{\log P_{1}}+\bar{\iota}_{2}\frac{\mathcal{G}_{j7}(P_{2}/x)}{\log P_{2}}\bigg)\,\frac{dx}{x}

\displaystyle+\frac{\mathfrak{a}}{(\log P_{1})^{2}}\int_{P_{2}}^{P_{1}}\frac{\mathcal{F}_{j6}(P_{1}/x)\mathcal{G}_{j6}(P_{1}/x)}{x}\,dx+o(\alpha).

This yields, by the change of variable
x\to P_{1}/x
or
x\to P_{2}/x
,

[formula]

\displaystyle S_{j}(\mathbb{a}_{11},\mathbb{a}_{21})=

\displaystyle\frac{\mathfrak{a}}{(\log P_{1})^{2}}\int_{1}^{P_{1}}\frac{\mathcal{F}_{j6}(x)\mathcal{G}_{j6}(x)}{x}\,dx+\frac{\mathfrak{a}|\iota_{2}|^{2}}{(\log P_{2})^{2}}\int_{1}^{P_{2}}\frac{\mathcal{F}_{j7}(x)\mathcal{G}_{j7}(x)}{x}\,dx

\displaystyle+\frac{\mathfrak{a}\iota_{2}}{(\log P_{1})(\log P_{2})}\int_{1}^{P_{2}}\frac{\mathcal{F}_{j7}(x)\mathcal{G}_{j6}(P^{0.004}T^{10}x)}{x}\,dx

\displaystyle+\frac{\mathfrak{a}\bar{\iota}_{2}}{(\log P_{1})(\log P_{2})}\int_{1}^{P_{2}}\frac{\mathcal{F}_{j6}(P^{0.004}T^{10}x)\mathcal{G}_{j7}(x)}{x}\,dx+o(\alpha),

since
P_{1}/P_{2}=P^{0.004}T^{10}
. By direct calculation, for
0\leq z\leq 1
we have

\mathcal{F}_{j6}(P^{z})=\mathfrak{f}_{j6}(z)+O(\mathcal{L}^{-8}),\qquad\mathcal{G}_{j6}(P^{z})=\mathfrak{g}_{j6}(z)+O(\mathcal{L}^{-8}),

\mathcal{F}_{j7}(P^{z})=\mathfrak{f}_{j7}(z)+O(\mathcal{L}^{-8}),\qquad\mathcal{G}_{j7}(P^{z})=\mathfrak{g}_{j7}(z)+O(\mathcal{L}^{-8})

where

[formula]

\mathfrak{f}_{16}(z)=\bigg(1+\frac{\pi i}{2}z\bigg)e^{(3\pi i/2)z},\quad\mathfrak{g}_{16}(z)=\frac{8}{3}+\bigg(-\frac{5}{3}-\frac{\pi i}{2}z\bigg)e^{(-3\pi i/2)z},

[formula]

\mathfrak{f}_{26}(z)=\bigg(1-\frac{\pi i}{2}z\bigg)e^{(3\pi i/2)z},\quad\mathfrak{g}_{26}(z)=\frac{4}{3}+\bigg(-\frac{1}{3}+\frac{\pi i}{2}z\bigg)e^{(-3\pi i/2)z},

[formula]

\mathfrak{f}_{36}(z)=\bigg(1-\frac{3\pi i}{2}z\bigg)e^{(3\pi i/2)z},\quad\mathfrak{g}_{36}(z)=\frac{8}{9}+\bigg(\frac{1}{9}+\frac{\pi i}{6}z\bigg)e^{(-3\pi i/2)z}.

[formula]

\mathfrak{f}_{17}(z)=\bigg(1+\frac{3\pi i}{2}z\bigg)e^{(5\pi i/2)z},\quad\mathfrak{g}_{17}(z)=\frac{24}{25}+\bigg(\frac{1}{25}+\frac{\pi i}{10}z\bigg)e^{(-5\pi i/2)z},

[formula]

\mathfrak{f}_{27}(z)=\bigg(1+\frac{\pi i}{2}z\bigg)e^{(5\pi i/2)z},\quad\mathfrak{g}_{27}(z)=\frac{12}{25}+\bigg(\frac{13}{25}+\frac{3\pi i}{10}z\bigg)e^{(-5\pi i/2)z},

[formula]

\mathfrak{f}_{37}(z)=\bigg(1-\frac{\pi i}{2}z\bigg)e^{(5\pi i/2)z},\quad\mathfrak{g}_{37}(z)=\frac{8}{25}+\bigg(\frac{17}{25}-\frac{3\pi i}{10}z\bigg)e^{(-5\pi i/2)z}.

Substituting
x=P^{z}
we obtain

\frac{1}{(\log P_{1})^{2}}\int_{1}^{P_{1}}\frac{\mathcal{F}_{j6}(x)\mathcal{G}_{j6}(x)}{x}\,dx=\frac{1}{(0.504)^{2}\log P}\int_{0}^{0.504}\mathfrak{f}_{j6}(z)\mathfrak{g}_{j6}(z)\,dz+o(\alpha),

\frac{1}{(\log P_{2})^{2}}\int_{1}^{P_{2}}\frac{\mathcal{F}_{j7}(x)\mathcal{G}_{j7}(x)}{x}\,dx=\frac{1}{(0.5)^{2}\log P}\int_{0}^{0.5}\mathfrak{f}_{j7}(z)\mathfrak{g}_{j7}(z)\,dz+o(\alpha),

\displaystyle\frac{1}{(\log P_{1})(\log P_{2})}

\displaystyle\int_{1}^{P_{2}}\frac{\mathcal{F}_{j7}(x)\mathcal{G}_{j6}(P^{0.004}T^{10}x)}{x}\,dx

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{(0.5)(0.504)\log P}\int_{0}^{0.5}\mathfrak{f}_{j7}(z)\mathfrak{g}_{j6}(z+0.004)\,dz+o(\alpha),

\displaystyle\frac{1}{(\log P_{1})(\log P_{2})}

\displaystyle\int_{1}^{P_{2}}\frac{\mathcal{F}_{j6}(P^{0.004}T^{10}x)\mathcal{G}_{j7}(x))}{x}\,dx

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{(0.5)(0.504)\log P}\int_{0}^{0.5}\mathfrak{f}_{j6}(z+0.004)\mathfrak{g}_{j7}(z)\,dz+o(\alpha).

Inserting these into (8.13) we obtain

\frac{1}{2\alpha}S_{1}(\mathbb{a}_{11},\mathbb{a}_{21})+\frac{2}{\alpha}S_{2}(\mathbb{a}_{11},\mathbb{a}_{21})+\frac{3}{2\alpha}S_{3}(\mathbb{a}_{11},\mathbb{a}_{21})=\mathfrak{a}\big(b_{11}+\iota_{2}b_{21}+\bar{\iota}_{2}b_{12}+|\iota_{2}|^{2}b_{22}\big)+o(1)

where

[formula]

b_{11}=\frac{1}{0.504^{2}\pi}\int_{0}^{0.504}\bigg(\frac{1}{2}\mathfrak{f}_{16}(z)\mathfrak{g}_{16}(z)+2\mathfrak{f}_{26}(z)\mathfrak{g}_{26}(z)+\frac{3}{2}\mathfrak{f}_{36}(z)\mathfrak{g}_{36}(z)\bigg)\,dz,

[formula]

b_{22}=\frac{1}{0.5^{2}\pi}\int_{0}^{0.5}\bigg(\frac{1}{2}\mathfrak{f}_{17}(z)\mathfrak{g}_{17}(z)+2\mathfrak{f}_{27}(z)\mathfrak{g}_{27}(z)+\frac{3}{2}\mathfrak{f}_{37}(z)\mathfrak{g}_{37}(z)\bigg)\,dz,

[formula]

\displaystyle b_{21}=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{(0.504)(0.5)\pi}

\displaystyle\times\int_{0}^{0.5}\bigg(\frac{1}{2}\mathfrak{f}_{17}(z)\mathfrak{g}_{16}(z+0.004)+2\mathfrak{f}_{27}(z)\mathfrak{g}_{26}(z+0.004)+\frac{3}{2}\mathfrak{f}_{37}(z)\mathfrak{g}_{36}(z+0.004)\bigg)\,dz.

[formula]

\displaystyle b_{12}=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{(0.504)(0.5)\pi}

\displaystyle\times\int_{0}^{0.5}\bigg(\frac{1}{2}\mathfrak{f}_{16}(z+0.004)\mathfrak{g}_{17}(z)+2\mathfrak{f}_{26}(z+0.004)\mathfrak{g}_{27}(z)+\frac{3}{2}\mathfrak{f}_{36}(z+0.004)\mathfrak{g}_{37}(z)\bigg)\,dz.

It follows by Proposition 7.1 that

\Theta_{1}(\mathbb{a}_{11},\mathbb{a}_{21})=\big(b_{11}+\iota_{2}b_{21}+\overline{\iota_{2}}b_{12}+|\iota_{2}|^{2}b_{22}\big)\mathfrak{a}\mathcal{P}+o(\mathcal{P}).

This yields, by (8.7),

[formula]

\Xi_{11}=\mathfrak{c}_{1}\mathfrak{a}\mathcal{P}+o(\mathcal{P})

where

\mathfrak{c}_{1}=c_{11}+\iota_{2}c_{21}+\overline{\iota_{2}}c_{12}+|\iota_{2}|^{2}c_{22}

with

c_{11}=b_{11}+\overline{b_{11}},

c_{22}=b_{22}+\overline{b_{22}},

c_{12}=b_{12}+\overline{b_{21}},

c_{21}=\overline{c_{12}}.

Let
\epsilon
be a complex number satisfying
|\epsilon|<10^{-5}
, not necessarily the same in each occurrence.
Numerical calculation shows that

c_{11}=3.61226+\epsilon/2,

c_{22}=1.32215+\epsilon/2,

c_{12}=-0.45757-0.18179i+\epsilon/\sqrt{2}.

It follows that

[formula]

\mathfrak{c}_{1}<6.9955.

9. Evaluation of
\Xi_{12}

In this section we evaluate
\Xi_{12}
. By Lemma 8.1,

[formula]

\Xi_{12}=2\Re\{\Theta_{1}(\mathbb{a}_{12},\mathbb{a}_{22})\}+o(\mathcal{P})

with
with

[formula]

a_{12}(n)=\chi(n)\big(\bar{\iota}_{3}{\varkappa}_{3}(n)+\bar{\iota}_{4}\varkappa_{2}(n)\big),\quad a_{22}(n)=\overline{a_{21}(n)}.

Hence

\displaystyle S_{j}(\mathbb{a}_{12},\mathbb{a}_{22})=

\displaystyle\sum_{d}\,\frac{|\chi(d)||\mu\chi(r)|\lambda_{0j}(dr)}{dr\varphi(r)}\bigg(\sum_{m}\frac{\chi(m)\big(\bar{\iota}_{3}\varkappa_{3}(drm)+\bar{\iota}_{4}\varkappa_{2}(drm)\big)}{m^{1-\beta_{j}}}\bigg)

\displaystyle\times\bigg(\sum_{n}\frac{\chi(n)\big(\iota_{3}\bar{\varkappa}_{3}(drn)+\iota_{4}\bar{\varkappa}_{2}(drn)\big)\xi_{0j}(n;d,r)}{n}\bigg).

By Lemma 8.2 and 8.4, for
dr<P_{3}/T
,

\sum_{m}\frac{\chi(m)\varkappa_{3}(drm)}{m^{1-\beta_{j}}}=\frac{L^{\prime}(1,\chi)}{\log P_{3}}\mathcal{F}_{j6}(P_{3}/dr)+O(\mathcal{L}^{-6})

and

\sum_{n}\frac{\chi(n)\bar{\varkappa}_{3}(drn)\xi_{0j}(n;d,r)}{n}=\frac{L^{\prime}(1,\chi)\Pi(d,r)}{\log P_{3}}\mathcal{G}_{j6}(P_{3}/dr)+O(\mathcal{L}^{-6}).

Thus, in a way similar to the proof of (8.12), we deduce that

\displaystyle S_{j}(\mathbb{a}_{12},\mathbb{a}_{22})=

\displaystyle\mathfrak{a}\sum_{r<D}\int_{1}^{P_{3}}\bigg(\bar{\iota}_{3}\frac{\mathcal{F}_{j6}(P_{3}/x)}{\log P_{3}}+\bar{\iota}_{4}\frac{\mathcal{F}_{j7}(P_{2}/x)}{\log P_{2}}\bigg)\bigg(\iota_{3}\frac{\mathcal{G}_{j6}(P_{3}/x)}{\log P_{3}}+\iota_{4}\frac{\mathcal{G}_{j7}(P_{2}/x)}{\log P_{2}}\bigg)\,\frac{dx}{x}

\displaystyle+\frac{|\iota_{4}|^{2}\mathfrak{a}}{(\log P_{2})^{2}}\int_{P_{3}}^{P_{2}}\mathcal{F}_{j7}(P_{2}/x)\mathcal{G}_{j7}(P_{2}/x)\,\frac{dx}{x}+o(\alpha).

It follows, by the change of variables
x\to P_{2}/x
or
x\to P_{3}/x
, that

\displaystyle S_{j}(\mathbb{a}_{12},\mathbb{a}_{22})=

\displaystyle\frac{\mathfrak{a}|\iota_{3}|^{2}}{(\log P_{3})^{2}}\int_{1}^{P_{3}}\frac{\mathcal{F}_{j6}(x)\mathcal{G}_{j6}(x)}{x}\,dx+\frac{\mathfrak{a}|\iota_{4}|^{2}}{(\log P_{2})^{2}}\int_{1}^{P_{2}}\frac{\mathcal{F}_{j7}(x)\mathcal{G}_{j7}(x)}{x}\,dx

\displaystyle+\frac{\mathfrak{a}\bar{\iota}_{3}\iota_{4}}{(\log P_{2})(\log P_{3})}\int_{1}^{P_{3}}\frac{\mathcal{F}_{j6}(x)\mathcal{G}_{j7}(P^{0.002}T^{-10}x)}{x}\,dx

\displaystyle+\frac{\mathfrak{a}\bar{\iota}_{4}\iota_{3}}{(\log P_{2})(\log P_{3})}\int_{1}^{P_{3}}\frac{\mathcal{F}_{j7}(P^{0.002}T^{-10}x)\mathcal{G}_{j6}(x)}{x}\,dx+o(\alpha),

since
P_{2}/P_{3}=P^{0.002}T^{-10}
. This yields, by substituting
x=P^{z}
,

\frac{1}{2\alpha}S_{1}(\mathbb{a}_{12},\mathbb{a}_{22})+\frac{2}{\alpha}S_{2}(\mathbb{a}_{12},\mathbb{a}_{22})+\frac{3}{2\alpha}S_{3}(\mathbb{a}_{12},\mathbb{a}_{22})=\mathfrak{a}\big(|\iota_{3}|^{2}b_{33}+\iota_{3}\bar{\iota}_{4}b_{34}+\iota_{4}\bar{\iota_{3}}b_{43}+|\iota_{4}|^{2}b_{44}\big)+o(1)

where

[formula]

b_{33}=\frac{1}{0.498^{2}\pi}\int_{0}^{0.498}\bigg(\frac{1}{2}\mathfrak{f}_{16}(z)\mathfrak{g}_{16}(z)+2\mathfrak{f}_{26}(z)\mathfrak{g}_{26}(z)+\frac{3}{2}\mathfrak{f}_{36}(z)\mathfrak{g}_{36}(z)\bigg)\,dz,

[formula]

b_{44}=b_{22},

[formula]

b_{34}=\frac{1}{(0.504)(0.498)\pi}\int_{0}^{0.498}\bigg(\frac{1}{2}\mathfrak{f}_{17}(z+0.002)\mathfrak{g}_{16}(z)+2\mathfrak{f}_{27}(z+0.002)\mathfrak{g}_{26}(z)+\frac{3}{2}\mathfrak{f}_{37}(z+0.002)\mathfrak{g}_{36}(z)\bigg)\,dz,

[formula]

b_{43}=\frac{1}{(0.504)(0.498)\pi}\int_{0}^{0.498}\bigg(\frac{1}{2}\mathfrak{f}_{16}(z)\mathfrak{g}_{17}(z+0.002)+2\mathfrak{f}_{26}(z)\mathfrak{g}_{27}(z+0.002)+\frac{3}{2}\mathfrak{f}_{36}(z)\mathfrak{g}_{37}(z+0.002)\bigg)\,dz.

It follows by Proposition 7.1 that

\Theta_{1}(\mathbb{a}_{12},\mathbb{a}_{22})=\big(|\iota_{3}|^{2}b_{33}+\iota_{3}\bar{\iota}_{4}b_{34}+\iota_{4}\bar{\iota}_{3}b_{43}+|\iota_{4}|^{2}b_{44}\big)\mathfrak{a}\mathcal{P}+o(\mathcal{P})

This yields

[formula]

\Xi_{12}=\mathfrak{c}_{2}\mathfrak{a}\mathcal{P}+o(\mathcal{P})

where

\mathfrak{c}_{2}=|\iota_{3}|^{2}c_{33}+\iota_{3}\bar{\iota}_{4}c_{34}+\iota_{4}\bar{\iota}_{3}c_{43}+|\iota_{4}|^{2}c_{44}

with

c_{33}=b_{33}+\overline{b_{33}},

c_{44}=c_{22},

c_{34}=b_{34}+\overline{b_{43}},

c_{43}=\overline{c_{34}}.

Numerical calculation shows that

c_{33}=3.69507+\epsilon/2,

c_{34}=-0.4526+0.19474i+\epsilon/\sqrt{2}.

It follows that

[formula]

\mathfrak{c}_{2}<6.9955.

10. Proof of Proposition 2.4

The goal of this section is to prove Proposition 2.4. We continue to assume
1\leq j\leq 3
.

Note that
\tilde{f}(z)\in\mathbf{R}
. If
\sigma=1/2
, then

\overline{J_{1}(s,\psi)}=\sum_{n}\frac{\chi\bar{\psi}(n)}{n^{1-s}}\tilde{f}\bigg(\frac{\log n}{\log P}\bigg)=J_{1}(1-s,\bar{\psi}),

\overline{J_{2}(s,\psi)}=\sum_{n}\frac{\chi\bar{\psi}(n)}{n^{1-s}}\tilde{f}\bigg(\frac{\log n}{\log P}+0.004-\tilde{\alpha}\bigg)=J_{2}(1-s,\bar{\psi}).

By Lemma 8.1 we have

\sum_{\psi\in\Psi_{1}}\,\sum_{\rho\in\mathcal{Z}(\psi)}\mathcal{C}^{*}(\rho,\psi)H_{1}(\rho,\psi)\overline{J_{1}(\rho,\psi)}\omega(\rho)=\Theta_{1}(\mathbb{a}_{11},\mathbb{a}_{13})+\overline{\Theta_{1}(\mathbb{a}_{13},\mathbb{a}_{21})}+o(\mathcal{P})

with
a_{11}(n)
and
a_{21}(n)
given by (8.8), and with

a_{13}(n)=\chi(n)\tilde{f}\bigg(\frac{\log n}{\log P}\bigg),

and

\sum_{\psi\in\Psi_{1}}\,\sum_{\rho\in\mathcal{Z}(\psi)}\mathcal{C}^{*}(\rho,\psi)\overline{H_{2}(\rho,\psi)}J_{2}(\rho,\psi)\omega(\rho)=\Theta_{1}(\mathbb{a}_{14},\mathbb{a}_{22})+\overline{\Theta_{1}(\mathbb{a}_{12},\mathbb{a}_{14})}+o(\mathcal{P})

with
a_{12}(n)
and
a_{22}(n)
given by (9.2), and with

a_{14}(n)=\chi(n)\tilde{f}\bigg(\frac{\log n}{\log P}+0.004-\tilde{\alpha}\bigg),

Hence

[formula]

\Xi_{1}^{*}=\Theta_{1}(\mathbb{a}_{11},\mathbb{a}_{13})+\overline{\Theta_{1}(\mathbb{a}_{13},\mathbb{a}_{21})}+\Theta_{1}(\mathbb{a}_{14},\mathbb{a}_{22})+\overline{\Theta_{1}(\mathbb{a}_{12},\mathbb{a}_{14})}+o(\mathcal{P}).

Lemma 10.1.

Write

\mathcal{V}_{1j}(y)=\sum_{m}\frac{\chi(m)\tilde{f}\big(\log(ym)/\log P\big)}{m^{1-\beta_{j}}}.

If
1\leq y\leq P^{0.5}/T
, then

[formula]

\mathcal{V}_{1j}(y)\ll T^{-c};

if
P^{0.5}<y\leq P^{0.502}/T
, then

[formula]

\mathcal{V}_{1j}(y)=\frac{500L^{\prime}(1,\chi)}{\log P}\big(-1-\beta_{j}\log(y/P^{0.5})\big)+O(\mathcal{L}^{-15});

if
P^{0.502}<y\leq P^{0.504}/T
, then

[formula]

\mathcal{V}_{1j}(y)=\frac{500L^{\prime}(1,\chi)}{\log P}\big(1-\beta_{j}\log(P^{0.504}/y)\big)+O(\mathcal{L}^{-15});

if

y\in(P^{0.5}/T,\,P^{0.5}]\cup(P^{0.502}/T,\,P^{0.502}]\cup(P^{0.504}/T,\,P^{0.504}),

then

[formula]

\mathcal{V}_{1j}(y)\ll\mathcal{L}^{-7}.

Proof.
The inequalities (10.2) follow by the Polya-Vinogradov inequality and partial summation.
Write

P_{1}^{\prime}=P^{0.504},\quad P_{2}^{\prime}=P^{0.502},\quad P_{3}^{\prime}=P^{0.5}.

Then

[formula]

\tilde{f}\big(\log y/\log P\big)=\frac{500}{\log P}\cdot\frac{1}{2\pi i}\int_{(1)}\frac{(P_{1}^{\prime})^{s}-2(P_{2}^{\prime})^{s}+(P_{3}^{\prime})^{s}}{y^{s}}\,\frac{ds}{s^{2}}.

In the case
P^{0.5}<y\leq P^{0.502}/T
,

[formula]

\mathcal{V}_{1j}(y)=\frac{500}{\log P}\cdot\frac{1}{2\pi i}\int_{(1)}L(1-\beta_{j}+s,\chi)\frac{(P_{1}^{\prime})^{s}-2(P_{2}^{\prime})^{s}}{y^{s}}\,\frac{ds}{s^{2}}.

by (10.6). The contour of integration is moved in the same way as in the proof of lemma 8.1. Hence, by Lemma 5.8, the right side above is equal to

\frac{500L^{\prime}(1,\chi)}{\log P}\cdot\frac{1}{2\pi i}\int_{|s|=5\alpha}\frac{(P_{1}^{\prime})^{s}-2(P_{2}^{\prime})^{s}}{y^{s}}\,\frac{(s-\beta_{j})ds}{s^{2}}+o(\mathcal{L}^{-15}).

The yields (11.3) since the residue of the function

\frac{(P_{1}^{\prime})^{s}-2(P_{2}^{\prime})^{s}}{y^{s}}\frac{s-\beta_{j}}{s^{2}}

at
s=0
is equal to
-1-\beta_{j}\log(y/P^{0.5})
.

Similarly, in the case
P^{0.502}<y\leq P^{0.504}/T
we have

\mathcal{V}_{1j}(y)=\frac{500}{\log P}\cdot\frac{1}{2\pi i}\int_{(1)}L(1-\beta_{j}+s,\chi)\bigg(\frac{P_{1}^{\prime}}{y}\bigg)^{s}\,\frac{ds}{s^{2}}.

This yields (10.4) in a way similar to the proof of (10.3).

In the case
y\in(P^{0.5}/T,\,P^{0.5}]\cup(P^{0.504}/T,\,P^{0.504})
and
m<T
,
we have

\tilde{f}\big(\log(ym)/\log P\big)\ll\alpha_{1},

so (10.5)
follows by the Polya-Vinogradov inequality and partial summation; in the case
y\in(P^{0.502}/T,\,P^{0.502}]
, we use (10.7) and a simple estimate for the integral on the new segments to obtain (10.5).
\Box

Lemma 10.2.

Write

\mathcal{V}_{2j}(d,r)=\sum_{n}\frac{\chi(n)\tilde{f}\big(\log(drn)/\log P\big)\xi_{0j}(n;d,r)}{n}.

If
dr\leq P^{0.5}/T
, then

[formula]

\mathcal{V}_{2j}(d,r)=\frac{L^{\prime}(1,\chi)\Pi(d,r)}{500}\beta_{j+1}\beta_{j+2}\log P+O(\mathcal{L}^{-15});

if
P^{0.5}<dr\leq P^{0.502}/T
, then

[formula]

\mathcal{V}_{2j}(d,r)=\frac{500L^{\prime}(1,\chi)\Pi(d,r)}{\log P}\big(-1+\mathcal{Y}_{1j}(dr)\big)+O(\mathcal{L}^{-15})

with

\mathcal{Y}_{1j}(y)=(\beta_{j+1}+\beta_{j+2})\log(y/P^{0.5})+\frac{\beta_{j+1}\beta_{j+2}}{2}\bigg(\log^{2}\frac{P^{0.504}}{y}-2\log^{2}\frac{P^{0.502}}{y}\bigg);

if
P^{0.502}<dr\leq P^{0.504}/T
, then

[formula]

\mathcal{V}_{2j}(d,r)=\frac{500L^{\prime}(1,\chi)\Pi(d,r)}{\log P}\big(1+\mathcal{Y}_{2j}(dr)\big)+O(\mathcal{L}^{-15})

with

\mathcal{Y}_{2j}(y)=(\beta_{j+1}+\beta_{j+2})\log(P^{0.504}/y)+\frac{\beta_{j+1}\beta_{j+2}}{2}\log^{2}(P^{0.504}/y);

if

dr\in(P^{0.5}/T,\,P^{0.5}]\cup(P^{0.502}/T,\,P^{0.502}]\cup(P^{0.504}/T,\,P^{0.504}),

then

[formula]

\mathcal{V}_{2j}(d,r)\ll\mathcal{L}^{-7}.

Proof.
First assume
dr\leq P^{0.5}/T
. In view of (8.9) and (10.6), we have

\displaystyle\mathcal{V}_{2j}(d,r)=\frac{500}{\log P}

\displaystyle\cdot\int_{(1)}\frac{L(1+\beta_{j+1}+s,\chi)L(1+\beta_{j+2}+s,\chi)\mathcal{U}_{0j}(s;d,r)}{L(1+s,\chi)}

\displaystyle\times\frac{(P_{1}^{\prime})^{s}-2(P_{2}^{\prime})^{s}+(P_{3}^{\prime})^{s}}{(dr)^{s}}\,\frac{ds}{s^{2}}.

The contour of integration is moved in the same way as the proof of Lemma 8.1. Thus, by Lemma 5.8 and 8.2,

\displaystyle\mathcal{V}_{2j}(d,r)=\frac{500L^{\prime}(1,\chi)\Pi(d,r)}{\log P}\cdot\int_{|s|=10\alpha}\frac{(\beta_{j+1}+s)(\beta_{j+2}+s)}{s}\frac{(P_{1}^{\prime})^{s}-2(P_{2}^{\prime})^{s}+(P_{3}^{\prime})^{s}}{(dr)^{s}}\,\frac{ds}{s^{2}}+O(\mathcal{L}^{-15}).

This yields (10.8) since the function

\frac{(P_{1}^{\prime})^{s}-2(P_{2}^{\prime})^{s}+(P_{3}^{\prime})^{s}}{s^{2}}

is analytic and equal to
(\log P/500)^{2}
at
s=0
.

Similarly, in the case
P^{0.5}/T<dr\leq P^{0.502}
we have

\displaystyle\mathcal{V}_{2j}(d,r)=\frac{500L^{\prime}(1,\chi)\Pi(d,r)}{\log P}\cdot\frac{1}{2\pi i}\int_{|s|=10\alpha}\frac{(\beta_{j+1}+s)(\beta_{j+2}+s)}{s}\frac{(P_{1}^{\prime})^{s}-2(P_{2}^{\prime})^{s}}{(dr)^{s}}\,\frac{ds}{s^{2}}+O(\mathcal{L}^{-15});

in the case
P^{0.502}<dr\leq P^{0.504}/T
we have

\displaystyle\mathcal{V}_{2j}(d,r)=\frac{500L^{\prime}(1,\chi)\Pi(d,r)}{\log P}\cdot\frac{1}{2\pi i}\int_{|s|=10\alpha}\frac{(\beta_{j+1}+s)(\beta_{j+2}+s)}{s}\bigg(\frac{P_{1}^{\prime}}{dr}\bigg)^{s}\,\frac{ds}{s^{2}}+O(\mathcal{L}^{-15}).

These yield (10.9) and (10.10). The proof of (10.11) is similar to that of (10.5) in the case
y\in(P^{0.502}/T,\,P^{0.502}]
.
\Box

By direct calculation, for
0\leq z\leq 1
we have

\beta_{j}\log P^{z-0.5}=\pi ij(z-0.5)+O(\mathcal{L}^{-8}),\quad\beta_{j}\log P^{0.504-z}=\pi ij(0.504-z)+O(\mathcal{L}^{-8}),

\mathcal{Y}_{1j}(P^{z})=\mathfrak{y}_{1j}(z)+O(\mathcal{L}^{-8}),\quad\mathcal{Y}_{2j}(P^{z})=\mathfrak{y}_{2j}(z)+O(\mathcal{L}^{-8}),

where

\mathfrak{y}_{11}(z)=5\pi i(z-0.5)-3\pi^{2}\big((0.504-z)^{2}-2(0.502-z)^{2}\big),

\mathfrak{y}_{21}(z)=5\pi i(0.504-z)-3\pi^{2}(0.504-z)^{2},

\mathfrak{y}_{12}(z)=4\pi i(z-0.5)-\frac{3\pi^{2}}{2}\big((0.504-z)^{2}-2(0.502-z)^{2}\big),

\mathfrak{y}_{22}(z)=4\pi i(0.504-z)-\frac{3\pi^{2}}{2}(0.504-z)^{2},

\mathfrak{y}_{13}(z)=3\pi i(z-0.5)-\pi^{2}\big((0.504-z)^{2}-2(0.502-z)^{2}\big),

\mathfrak{y}_{23}(z)=3\pi i(0.504-z)-\pi^{2}\big((0.504-z)^{2}.

Remark.
Similar to (10.6),

\tilde{f}\big(\log y/\log P+0.004-\tilde{\alpha}\big)=\frac{500}{\log P}\cdot\frac{1}{2\pi i}\int_{(1)}\bigg(\frac{Dt_{0}}{P^{0.004}}\bigg)^{s}\frac{(P_{1}^{\prime})^{s}-2(P_{2}^{\prime})^{s}+(P_{3}^{\prime})^{s}}{y^{s}}\,\frac{ds}{s^{2}}.

Thus, with simple modification, Lemma 10.1 and 10.2 apply to the sums

\sum_{m}\frac{\chi(m)\tilde{f}\big(\log(ym)/\log P+0.004-\tilde{\alpha}\big)}{m^{1-\beta_{j}}}

and

\sum_{n}\frac{\chi(n)\tilde{f}\big(\log(drn)/\log P+0.004-\tilde{\alpha}\big)\xi_{0j}(n;d,r)}{n}.

Evaluation of
\Theta_{1}(\mathbb{a}_{11},\mathbb{a}_{13})
.

We have

\displaystyle S_{j}(\mathbb{a}_{11},\mathbb{a}_{13})=

\displaystyle\sum_{r}\,\sum_{d}\frac{|\chi(d)||\mu\chi(r)|\lambda_{0j}(dr)}{dr\varphi(r)}\bigg(\sum_{m}\frac{\chi(m)\big(\varkappa_{1}(drm)+\iota_{2}\varkappa_{2}(drm)\big)}{m^{1-\beta_{j}}}\bigg)

\displaystyle\times\bigg(\sum_{n}\frac{\chi(n)\tilde{f}\big(\log(drn)/\log P\big)\xi_{0j}(n;d,r)}{n}\bigg).

The right side is split into three sums according to

dr<P^{0.5},\quad P^{0.5}\leq dr<P^{0.502},\quad P^{0.502}\leq dr<P^{0.504}.

By Lemma 10.2 and the results in Section 8, the sum over
dr<P^{0.5}
is equal to

\displaystyle\frac{L^{\prime}(1,\chi)^{2}}{500}

\displaystyle\beta_{j+1}\beta_{j+2}\sum_{n<P^{0.5}}\,\frac{|\chi(n)|\lambda_{0j}(n)}{\varphi(n)}\bigg(\frac{\mathcal{F}_{j6}(P^{0.504}/n)}{0.504}+\iota_{2}\frac{\mathcal{F}_{j7}(P^{0.5}/n)}{0.5}\bigg)+o(\alpha)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{\mathfrak{a}\beta_{j+1}\beta_{j+2}}{500}\int_{1}^{P^{0.5}}\bigg(\frac{\mathcal{F}_{j6}(P^{0.504}/x)}{0.504}+\iota_{2}\frac{\mathcal{F}_{j7}(P^{0.5}/x)}{0.5}\bigg)\,\frac{dx}{x}+o(\alpha)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{\mathfrak{a}\beta_{j+1}\beta_{j+2}\log P}{500}\int_{0}^{0.5}\bigg(\frac{\mathfrak{f}_{j6}(0.004+z)}{0.504}+\iota_{2}\frac{\mathfrak{f}_{j7}(z)}{0.5}\bigg)\,dz+o(\alpha).

Similarly, the sum over
P^{0.5}\leq dr<P^{0.502}
is equal to

\displaystyle\frac{500L^{\prime}(1,\chi)^{2}}{0.504\log^{2}P}

\displaystyle\sum_{P^{0.5}\leq n<P^{0.502}}\,\frac{|\chi(n)|\lambda_{0j}(n)}{\varphi(n)}\mathcal{F}_{j6}(P^{0.504}/n)\big(-1+\mathcal{Y}_{1j}(n)\big)+o(\alpha)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{500\mathfrak{a}}{0.504\log P}\int_{0.5}^{0.502}\mathfrak{f}_{j6}(0.504-z)\big(-1+\mathfrak{y}_{1j}(z)\big)\,dz+o(\alpha);

the sum over
P^{0.502}\leq dr<P^{0.504}
is equal to

\displaystyle\frac{500L^{\prime}(1,\chi)^{2}}{0.504\log^{2}P}

\displaystyle\sum_{P^{0.502}\leq n<P^{0.504}}\frac{|\chi(n)|\lambda_{0j}(n)}{\varphi(n)}\mathcal{F}_{j6}(P^{0.504}/n)\big(1+\mathcal{Y}_{2j}(n)\big)+o(\alpha)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{500\mathfrak{a}}{0.504\log P}\int_{0.502}^{0.504}\mathfrak{f}_{j6}(0.504-z)\big(1+\mathfrak{y}_{2j}(z)\big)\,dz+o(\alpha).

Noting that

\beta_{j+1}\beta_{j+2}\log P=-(11-6j+j^{2})\pi\alpha+o(\alpha)

and gathering the above results together we conclude

\frac{1}{\alpha}S_{j}(\mathbb{a}_{11},\mathbb{a}_{13})=d_{3j}\mathfrak{a}+o(1)

with

\displaystyle d_{3j}=

\displaystyle-\frac{(11-6j+j^{2})\pi}{500}\int_{0}^{0.5}\bigg(\frac{1}{0.504}\mathfrak{f}_{j6}(0.004+z)+\frac{\iota_{2}}{0.5}\mathfrak{f}_{j7}(z)\bigg)\,dz

\displaystyle+\frac{500}{0.504\pi}\int_{0}^{0.002}\big(\mathfrak{f}_{j6}(z)-\mathfrak{f}_{j6}(0.002+z)\big)\,dz

\displaystyle+\frac{500}{0.504\pi}\bigg(\int_{0.5}^{0.502}\mathfrak{f}_{j6}(0.504-z)\mathfrak{y}_{1j}(z)\,dz+\int_{0.502}^{0.504}\mathfrak{f}_{j6}(0.504-z)\mathfrak{y}_{2j}(z)\,dz\bigg).

Hence, by Proposition 7.1,

[formula]

\Theta_{1}(\mathbb{a}_{11},\mathbb{a}_{13})=\bigg(\frac{1}{2}d_{31}+2d_{32}+\frac{3}{2}d_{33}\bigg)\mathfrak{a}\mathcal{P}+o(\mathcal{P}).

Evaluation of
\Theta_{1}(\mathbb{a}_{13},\mathbb{a}_{21})
.

We have

\displaystyle S_{j}(\mathbb{a}_{13},\mathbb{a}_{21})=

\displaystyle\sum_{r}\,\sum_{d}\frac{|\chi(d)||\mu\chi(r)|\lambda_{0j}(dr)}{dr\varphi(r)}\bigg(\sum_{m}\frac{\chi(m)\tilde{f}\big(\log(drm)/\log P\big)}{m^{1-\beta_{j}}}\bigg)

\displaystyle\times\bigg(\sum_{n}\frac{\chi(n)\big(\bar{\varkappa}_{1}(drn)+\bar{\iota}_{2}\bar{\varkappa}_{2}(drn)\big)\xi_{0j}(n;d,r)}{n}\bigg).

The right side is split into three sums in the same way as in the last subsection.
By Lemma 10.1 and the results in Section 8, the sum over
dr<P^{0.5}
is
o(\alpha)
;
the sum over
P^{0.5}\leq dr<P^{0.502}
is equal to

\displaystyle\frac{500L^{\prime}(1,\chi)^{2}}{0.504\log^{2}P}\sum_{P^{0.5}\leq n<P^{0.502}}\frac{|\chi(n)|\lambda_{0j}(n)}{\varphi(n)}\big(-1-\beta_{j}\log(y/P^{0.5})\big)\mathcal{G}_{j6}(P^{0.504}/dr)+o(\alpha)

\displaystyle=\frac{500\mathfrak{a}}{0.504\log P}\int_{0.5}^{0.502}\big(-1-\pi ij(z-0.5)\big)\mathfrak{g}_{j6}(0.504-z)\,dz+o(\alpha);

the sum over
P^{0.502}<dr\leq P^{0.504}
is equal to

\displaystyle\frac{500L^{\prime}(1,\chi)^{2}}{0.504\log^{2}P}\sum_{P^{0.502}\leq n<P^{0.504}}\frac{|\chi(n)|\lambda_{0j}(n)}{\varphi(n)}\big(1-\beta_{j}\log(P^{0.504}/n)\big)\mathcal{G}_{j6}(P^{0.504}/n)+o(\alpha)

\displaystyle=\frac{500\mathfrak{a}}{0.504\log P}\int_{0.502}^{0.504}\big(1-\pi ij(0.504-z)\big)\mathfrak{g}_{j6}(0.504-z)\,dz+o(\alpha),

Gathering these results together we conclude

\frac{1}{\alpha}S_{j}(\mathbb{a}_{13},\mathbb{a}_{21})=d_{4j}\mathfrak{a}+o(1)

with

\displaystyle d_{4j}=

\displaystyle\frac{500}{0.504\pi}\int_{0}^{0.002}\big(\mathfrak{g}_{j6}(z)-\mathfrak{g}_{j6}(0.002+z)\big)\,dz

\displaystyle-

\displaystyle\frac{500ij}{0.504}\int_{0}^{0.002}\big(\mathfrak{g}_{j6}(0.002+z)(0.002-z)+\mathfrak{g}_{j6}(z)z\big)\,dz.

Hence, by Proposition 7.1,

[formula]

\Theta_{1}(\mathbb{a}_{13},\mathbb{a}_{21})=\bigg(\frac{1}{2}d_{41}+2d_{42}+\frac{3}{2}d_{43}\bigg)\mathfrak{a}\mathcal{P}+o(\mathcal{P}).

Evaluation of
\Theta_{1}(\mathbb{a}_{14},\mathbb{a}_{22})
.

We have

\displaystyle S_{j}(\mathbb{a}_{14},\mathbb{a}_{22})=

\displaystyle\sum_{r}\,\sum_{d}\frac{|\chi(d)||\mu\chi(r)|\lambda_{0j}(dr)}{dr\varphi(r)}\bigg(\sum_{m}\frac{\chi(m)\tilde{f}\big(\log(drm)/\log P+0.004-\tilde{\alpha}\big)}{m^{1-\beta_{j}}}\bigg)

\displaystyle\times\bigg(\sum_{n}\frac{\chi(n)\big(\iota_{3}\bar{\varkappa}_{3}(drn)+\iota_{4}\bar{\varkappa}_{2}(drn)\big)\xi_{0j}(n;d,r)}{n}\bigg).

The right side is split into three sums according to

dr<P^{0.496},\quad P^{0.496}\leq dr<P^{0.498},\quad P^{0.498}\leq dr<P^{0.5}.

By a result similar to Lemma 10.1 and the results in Section 8, the sum over
dr<P^{0.496}
is
o(\alpha)
; the sum over
P^{0.496}\leq dr<P^{0.498}
is equal to

\displaystyle\frac{500L^{\prime}(1,\chi)^{2}}{\log^{2}P}\sum_{P^{0.496}\leq n<P^{0.498}}\frac{|\chi(n)|\lambda_{0j}(n)}{\varphi(n)}\big(-1-\beta_{j}\log(P^{-0.496}n)\big)

\displaystyle\times\bigg(\frac{\iota_{3}}{0.498}\mathcal{G}_{j6}(P^{0.498}/n)+\frac{\iota_{4}}{0.5}\mathcal{G}_{j7}(P^{0.5}/n)\bigg)+o(\alpha)

\displaystyle=\frac{500\mathfrak{a}}{\log P}\int_{0.496}^{0.498}\big(-1-\pi ij(z-0.496)\big)

\displaystyle\times\bigg(\frac{\iota_{3}}{0.498}\mathfrak{g}_{j6}(0.498-z)+\frac{\iota_{4}}{0.5}\mathfrak{g}_{j7}(0.5-z)\big)\,dz+o(\alpha);

the sum over
P^{0.498}\leq dr<P^{0.5}
is equal to

\displaystyle\frac{500L^{\prime}(1,\chi)^{2}}{\log^{2}P}\frac{\iota_{4}}{0.5}\sum_{P^{0.498}\leq n<P^{0.5}}\frac{|\chi(n)|\lambda_{0j}(n)}{\varphi(n)}\big(1-\beta_{j}\log(P^{0.5}/n)\big)\mathcal{G}_{j7}(P^{0.5}/n)+o(\alpha)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1000\iota_{4}\mathfrak{a}}{\log P}\int_{0.498}^{0.5}\big(1-\pi ij(0.5-z)\big)\mathfrak{g}_{j7}(0.5-z)\,dz+o(\alpha).

Gathering the above results together we conclude

\frac{1}{\alpha}S_{j}(\mathbb{a}_{14},\mathbb{a}_{22})=(d_{5j}^{\prime}+d_{5j})\mathfrak{a}+o(1)

with

d_{5j}^{\prime}=-\frac{500\iota_{3}}{0.498\pi}\int_{0}^{0.002}\mathfrak{g}_{j6}(z)\,dz,

\displaystyle d_{5j}=

\displaystyle\frac{1000\iota_{4}}{\pi}\int_{0}^{0.002}\big(\mathfrak{g}_{j7}(z)-\mathfrak{g}_{j7}(0.002+z)\big)\,dz

\displaystyle-

\displaystyle\frac{500ij\iota_{3}}{0.498}\int_{0}^{0.002}(0.002-z)\mathfrak{g}_{j6}(z)\,dz

\displaystyle-

\displaystyle 1000ij\iota_{4}\int_{0}^{0.002}\big((0.002-z)\mathfrak{g}_{j7}(0.002+z)+z\mathfrak{g}_{j7}(z)\big)\,dz.

Hence, by Proposition 7.1,

[formula]

\Theta_{1}(\mathbb{a}_{14},\mathbb{a}_{22})=\bigg(\frac{1}{2}(d_{51}^{\prime}+d_{51})+2(d_{52}^{\prime}+d_{52})+\frac{3}{2}(d_{53}^{\prime}+d_{53})\bigg)\mathfrak{a}\mathcal{P}+o(\mathcal{P}).

Evaluation of
\Theta_{1}(\mathbb{a}_{12},\mathbb{a}_{14})
.

We have

\displaystyle S_{j}(\mathbb{a}_{12},\mathbb{a}_{14})=

\displaystyle\sum_{r}\,\sum_{d}\frac{|\chi(d)||\mu\chi(r)|\lambda_{0j}(dr)}{dr\varphi(r)}\bigg(\sum_{m}\frac{\chi(m)\big(\bar{\iota}_{3}\varkappa_{3}(drm)+\bar{\iota}_{4}\varkappa_{2}(drm)\big)}{m^{1-\beta_{j}}}\bigg)

\displaystyle\times\bigg(\sum_{n}\frac{\chi(n)\tilde{f}\big(\log(drn)/\log P+0.004-\tilde{\alpha}\big)\xi_{0j}(n;d,r)}{n}\bigg).

The right side is split into three sums in the same way as in the last subsection.
By a result similar to Lemma 10.2 and the results in Section 8, the sum over
dr<P^{0.496}
is equal to

\displaystyle\frac{L^{\prime}(1,\chi)^{2}}{500}

\displaystyle\beta_{j+1}\beta_{j+2}\sum_{n<P^{0.496}}\frac{|\chi(n)|\lambda_{0j}(n)}{\varphi(n)}\bigg(\frac{\bar{\iota}_{3}\mathcal{F}_{j6}(P^{0.498}/n)}{0.498}+\frac{\bar{\iota}_{4}\mathcal{F}_{j7}(P^{0.5}/n)}{0.5}\bigg)+o(\alpha)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{\mathfrak{a}\beta_{j+1}\beta_{j+2}}{500}\int_{1}^{P^{0.496}}\bigg(\frac{\bar{\iota}_{3}\mathcal{F}_{j6}(P^{0.498}/x)}{0.498}+\frac{\bar{\iota}_{4}\mathcal{F}_{j7}(P^{0.5}/x)}{0.5}\bigg)\,\frac{dx}{x}+o(\alpha)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{\mathfrak{a}\beta_{j+1}\beta_{j+2}\log P}{500}\int_{0}^{0.496}\bigg(\frac{\bar{\iota}_{3}\mathfrak{f}_{j6}(0.002+z)}{0.498}+\frac{\bar{\iota}_{4}\mathfrak{f}_{j7}(0.004+z)}{0.5}\bigg)\,dz+o(\alpha);

the sum over
P^{0.496}\leq dr<P^{0.498}
is equal to

\displaystyle\frac{500L^{\prime}(1,\chi)^{2}}{\log^{2}P}\sum_{p^{0.496}\leq n<P^{0.498}}\frac{|\chi(n)|\lambda_{0j}(n)}{\varphi(n)}\bigg(\frac{\bar{\iota}_{3}\mathcal{F}_{j6}(P^{0.498}/n)}{0.498}+\frac{\bar{\iota}_{4}\mathcal{F}_{j7}(P^{0.5}/n)}{0.5}\bigg)

\displaystyle\times\big(-1+\mathcal{Y}_{1j}(P^{0.004}n)\big)+o(\alpha)

\displaystyle=\frac{500\mathfrak{a}}{\log P}\int_{0}^{0.002}\bigg(\frac{\bar{\iota}_{3}\mathfrak{f}_{j6}(z)}{0.498}+\frac{\bar{\iota}_{4}\mathfrak{f}_{j7}(0.002+z)}{0.5}\bigg)\big(-1+\mathfrak{y}_{1j}(0.502-z)\big)\,dz+o(\alpha);

the sum over
P^{0.498}\leq dr<P^{0.5}
is equal to

\displaystyle\frac{1000\bar{\iota_{4}}L^{\prime}(1,\chi)^{2}}{\log^{2}P}\ \sum_{p^{0.498}\leq n<P^{0.5}}\frac{|\chi(n)|\lambda_{0j}(n)}{\varphi(n)}\mathcal{F}_{j7}(P^{0.5}/n)\big(1+\mathcal{Y}_{2j}(P^{0.004}n)\big)+o(\alpha)

\displaystyle=\frac{1000\bar{\iota_{4}}\mathfrak{a}}{\log P}\int_{0}^{0.002}\mathfrak{f}_{j7}(z)\big(1+\mathfrak{y}_{2j}(0.504-z)\big)\,dz+o(\alpha).

Gathering the above results together we conclude

\frac{1}{\alpha}S_{j}(\mathbb{a}_{12},\mathbb{a}_{14})=(d_{6j}^{\prime}+d_{6j})\mathfrak{a}+o(1)

with

[formula]

d_{6j}^{\prime}=-\frac{500\bar{\iota}_{3}}{0.498\pi}\int_{0}^{0.002}\mathfrak{f}_{j6}(z)\,dz,

\displaystyle d_{6j}=

\displaystyle-\frac{(11-6j+j^{2})\pi}{500}\int_{0}^{0.496}\bigg(\frac{\bar{\iota}_{3}\mathfrak{f}_{j6}(0.002+z)}{0.498}+\frac{\bar{\iota}_{4}\mathfrak{f}_{j7}(0.004+z)}{0.5}\bigg)\,dz

\displaystyle+\frac{1000\bar{\iota}_{4}}{\pi}\int_{0}^{0.002}\big(\mathfrak{f}_{j7}(z)-\mathfrak{f}_{j7}(0.002+z)\big)\,dz

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\frac{500}{\pi}\int_{0}^{0.002}\bigg(\frac{\bar{\iota}_{3}\mathfrak{f}_{j6}(z)}{0.498}+\frac{\bar{\iota}_{4}\mathfrak{f}_{j7}(0.002+z)}{0.5}\bigg)\mathfrak{y}_{1j}(0.502-z)\,dz

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\frac{1000\bar{\iota}_{4}}{\pi}\int_{0}^{0.002}\mathfrak{f}_{j7}(z)\mathfrak{y}_{2j}(0.504-z)\,dz.

Hence, by Proposition 7.1,

[formula]

\Theta_{1}(\mathbb{a}_{12},\mathbb{a}_{14})=\bigg(\frac{1}{2}(d_{61}^{\prime}+d_{61})+2(d_{62}^{\prime}+d_{62})+\frac{3}{2}d_{63}^{\prime}+d_{63})\bigg)\mathfrak{a}\mathcal{P}+o(\mathcal{P}).

It follows from (10.1) and (10.12)-(10.16) that

[formula]

\Xi_{1}^{*}=(\mathfrak{d}^{\prime}+\mathfrak{d})\mathfrak{a}\mathcal{P}+o(\mathcal{P})

where

\mathfrak{d}^{\prime}=\frac{1}{2}\big(d_{51}^{\prime}+\overline{d^{\prime}_{61}}\big)+2\big(d_{52}^{\prime}+\overline{d^{\prime}_{62}}\big)+\frac{3}{2}\big(d_{53}^{\prime}+\overline{d^{\prime}_{63}}\big),

\mathfrak{d}=\frac{1}{2}\big(d_{31}+d_{51}+\overline{d_{41}+d_{61}}\big)+2\big(d_{32}+d_{52}+\overline{d_{42}+d_{62}}\big)+\frac{3}{2}\big(d_{33}+d_{53}+\overline{d_{43}+d_{63}}\big)

For
\mu=6,7
,

\mathfrak{f}_{j\mu}(0)=\mathfrak{g}_{j\mu}(0)=1.

Hence

\mathfrak{d}^{\prime}\simeq-\frac{8\iota_{3}}{0.498\pi}.

We only need a crude lower bound for the real part of
\mathfrak{d}^{\prime}
. By direct calculation we have

\Re\{\mathfrak{d}^{\prime}\}>-\frac{8}{0.498\pi}\Re\{\iota_{3}\}-0.04>5.1.

The contribution from
\mathfrak{d}
is minor since if
z
is close to
1/2
, then for
\mu=1,2
,

\mathfrak{y}_{\mu j}(z)\simeq 0.

Thus, by direct calculation we have the crude bound

|\Re\{\mathfrak{d}\}|<0.1.

Combining these bounds with (10.16) we complete the proof of Proposition 2.4.
\Box

Remark.
Numerical calculation actually shows that

\Re\{\mathfrak{d}\}>0.

11. Proof of Proposition 2.6

By the result of Section 9,

\Xi_{12}\ll\mathfrak{a}\mathcal{P}.

Hence, by Cauchy’s inequality, the proof of Proposition 2.6 is reduced to showing that

[formula]

\sum_{\psi\in\Psi_{1}}\,\sum_{\rho\in\tilde{Z}(\psi)}\mathcal{C}^{*}(\rho,\psi)|J_{1}(\rho,\psi)-Z(\rho,\chi\psi)J_{2}(1-\rho,\bar{\psi})|^{2}\omega(\rho)=o(\mathfrak{a}\mathcal{P}).

Let
\tilde{g}_{1}(y)
and
\tilde{g}_{2}(y)
be given by

\tilde{g}_{1}(y)=-500\int_{0.5}^{0.502}g\bigg(\frac{P^{z}}{y}\bigg)\,dz+500\int_{0.502}^{0.504}g\bigg(\frac{P^{z}}{y}\bigg)\,dz,

\tilde{g}_{2}(y)=-500\int_{0.496}^{0.498}g\bigg(\frac{P^{z}Dt_{0}}{y}\bigg)\,dz+500\int_{0.498}^{0.5}g\bigg(\frac{P^{z}Dt_{0}}{y}\bigg)\,dz.

Write

\tilde{J}_{\mu}(s,\psi)=\sum_{n}\frac{\chi\psi(n)\tilde{g}_{\mu}(n)}{n^{s}},\quad\mu=1,2,

and

\eta_{\pm}=\exp\{\pm\mathcal{L}^{-10}\}.

By (5) and (5), for
\sigma=1/2
, the terms with
n\leq P^{0.5}\eta_{-}
or
n\geq P^{0.504}\eta_{+}
in
\tilde{J}_{1}(s,\psi)
contribute

\ll\varepsilon
.

Lemma 11.1.

If

y\in[P^{0.5}\eta_{+},\,P^{0.502}\eta_{-}]\cup[P^{0.502}\eta_{+},\,P^{0.504}\eta_{-}],

then

[formula]

\tilde{f}\bigg(\frac{\log y}{\log P}\bigg)-\tilde{g}_{1}(y)\ll\varepsilon;

if

y\in(P^{0.5}\eta_{-},\,,P^{0.5}\eta_{+})\cup(P^{0.502}\eta_{-},\,P^{0.502}\eta_{+})\cup(P^{0.504}\eta_{-},\,P^{0.504}\eta_{+}),

then

[formula]

\tilde{f}\bigg(\frac{\log y}{\log P}\bigg)-\tilde{g}_{1}(y)\ll\mathcal{L}^{-10}.

Proof.

Since

g(1/x)=\frac{1}{\sqrt{\pi}}\int_{-\infty}^{-\mathcal{L}\log x}\exp(-t^{2})\,dt=\frac{1}{\sqrt{\pi}}\int_{\mathcal{L}\log x}^{\infty}\exp(-t^{2})\,dt,

it follows that

[formula]

g(x)+g(1/x)=1.

Write

u=\frac{\log y}{\log P}.

First assume
T^{0.502}\eta_{+}\leq y\leq P^{0.503}
. By (11.4),

\int_{0.502}^{2u-0.502}g\bigg(\frac{P^{z}}{y}\bigg)\,dz=u-0.502;

by (6.2),

\int_{2u-0.502}^{0.504}g\bigg(\frac{P^{z}}{y}\bigg)\,dz=1.006-2u+O(\varepsilon);

by (6.3),

\int_{0.5}^{0.502}g\bigg(\frac{P^{z}}{y}\bigg)\,dz\ll\varepsilon.

These together imply (11.2).

Now assume
P^{0.503}\leq y\leq P^{0.504}\eta_{-}
. By (11.4),

\int_{2u-0.504}^{0.504}g\bigg(\frac{P^{z}}{y}\bigg)\,dz=0.504-u;

by (6.2),

\int_{0.502}^{2u-0.504}g\bigg(\frac{P^{z}}{y}\bigg)\,dz=2u-1.006+O(\varepsilon);

by (6.3),

\int_{0.5}^{0.502}g\bigg(\frac{P^{z}}{y}\bigg)\,dz\ll\varepsilon.

These together imply (11.2). The proof of (11.2) with
y\in[P^{0.5}\eta_{+},\,P^{0.502}\eta_{-}]
is similar.

We give the proof of (11.3) with
P^{0.502}\eta_{-}<y<P^{0.502}\eta_{+}
only; the other cases are similar. We have

\int_{0.502}^{0.504}g\bigg(\frac{P^{z}}{y}\bigg)\,dz=\frac{1}{500}+O(\mathcal{L}^{-10}),\quad\int_{0.5}^{0.502}g\bigg(\frac{P^{z}}{y}\bigg)\,dz=O(\mathcal{L}^{-10}),

by (5.2) and (5.3), and

\tilde{f}\bigg(\frac{\log y}{\log P}\bigg)=1+O(\mathcal{L}^{-10}).\quad\Box

It follows from Lemma 11.1 that

[formula]

J_{1}(s,\psi)-\tilde{J}_{1}(s,\psi)=\sum_{n\in\mathfrak{I}_{1}}\frac{\chi\psi(n)}{n^{s}}\big(\tilde{f}(\log n/\log P)-\tilde{g}_{1}(n)\big)+O(\varepsilon)

for
\sigma=1/2
, where

\mathfrak{I}_{1}=(P^{0.5}\eta_{-},\,P^{0.5}\eta_{+})\cup(P^{0.502}\eta_{-},\,P^{0.502}\eta_{+})\cup(P^{0.504}\eta_{-},\,P^{0.504}\eta_{+}).

By (11.3), (8.25) and (8.26),

\sum_{\psi\in\Psi_{1}}\,\sum_{\rho\in\tilde{Z}(\psi)}\mathcal{C}^{*}(\rho,\psi)\bigg|\sum_{n\in\mathfrak{I}_{1}}\frac{\chi\psi(n)}{n^{\rho}}\big(\tilde{f}(\log n/\log P)-\tilde{g}_{1}(n)\big)\bigg|^{2}\omega(\rho)=o(\mathfrak{a}\mathcal{P}).

Hence

\sum_{\psi\in\Psi_{1}}\,\sum_{\rho\in\tilde{Z}(\psi)}\mathcal{C}^{*}(\rho,\psi)\big|J_{1}(\rho,\psi)-\tilde{J}_{1}(\rho,\psi)\big|^{2}\omega(\rho)=o(\mathfrak{a}\mathcal{P}).

Similarly,

\sum_{\psi\in\Psi_{1}}\,\sum_{\rho\in\tilde{Z}(\psi)}\mathcal{C}^{*}(\rho,\psi)\big|J_{2}(\rho,\psi)-\tilde{J}_{2}(\rho,\psi)\big|^{2}\omega(\rho)=o(\mathfrak{a}\mathcal{P}).

The proof of (11.1) is therefore reduced to showing that

[formula]

\sum_{\psi\in\Psi_{1}}\,\sum_{\rho\in\tilde{Z}(\psi)}\mathcal{C}^{*}(\rho,\psi)\big|\tilde{J}_{1}(\rho,\psi)-Z(\rho,\chi\psi)\tilde{J}_{2}(1-\rho,\bar{\psi})\big|^{2}\omega(\rho)=o(\mathfrak{a}\mathcal{P}).

Lemma 11.2.

Suppose
\sigma=1/2
and
|t-2\pi t_{0}|<\mathcal{L}_{1}
. Then

\tilde{J}_{1}(s,\psi)=Z(s,\chi\psi)\tilde{J}_{2}(1-s,\bar{\psi})+O(E_{2}(s,\psi))

where

E(s,\psi)=\mathcal{L}^{-68}\int_{-\mathcal{L}^{20}}^{\mathcal{L}^{20}}\bigg|\sum_{n<P_{1}}\frac{\chi\psi(n)}{n^{s+iv}}\bigg|\omega_{1}(iv)\,dv.

Proof.
Suppose
0.5\leq z\leq 0.504
. In a way similar to the proof of Lemma 6.1, it can be deduced that

\sum_{n}\frac{\chi\psi(n)}{n^{s}}g\bigg(\frac{P^{z}}{n}\bigg)=L(s,\chi\psi)-Z(s,\chi\psi)\sum_{n}\frac{\chi\bar{\psi}(n)}{n^{1-s}}g\bigg(\frac{P^{1-z}Dt_{0}}{n}\bigg)+O(E_{2}(s,\psi)).

Hence

\displaystyle\int_{0.5}^{0.502}

\displaystyle\bigg\{\sum_{n}\frac{\chi\psi(n)}{n^{s}}g\bigg(\frac{P^{z}}{n}\bigg)\bigg\}\,dz

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{500}L(s,\chi\psi)-Z(s,\chi\psi)\int_{0.498}^{0.5}\bigg\{\sum_{n}\frac{\chi\bar{\psi}(n)}{n^{1-s}}g\bigg(\frac{P^{z}Dt_{0}}{n}\bigg)\bigg\}\,dz+O(E_{2}(s,\psi)),

and

\displaystyle\int_{0.502}^{0.504}

\displaystyle\bigg\{\sum_{n}\frac{\chi\psi(n)}{n^{s}}g\bigg(\frac{P^{z}}{n}\bigg)\bigg\}\,dz

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{500}L(s,\chi\psi)-Z(s,\chi\psi)\int_{0.496}^{0.498}\bigg\{\sum_{n}\frac{\chi\bar{\psi}(n)}{n^{1-s}}g\bigg(\frac{P^{z}Dt_{0}}{n}\bigg)\bigg\}\,dz+O(E_{2}(s,\psi)).

This completes the proof.
\Box

By Lemma 11.2, the proof of (11.6) is reduced to showing that

\sum_{\psi\in\Psi_{1}}\,\sum_{\rho\in\tilde{Z}(\psi)}\mathcal{C}^{*}(\rho,\psi)E_{2}(\rho,\psi)^{2}\omega(\rho)=o(\mathfrak{a}\mathcal{P}).

This follows by (8.25), (8.26) and simple estimates.

12. Evaluation of
\Xi_{15}

The sum
H_{11}(s,\psi)
is split into

[formula]

H_{11}(s,\psi)=\sum_{n<P^{1/2}}+\sum_{P^{1/2}\leq n<P_{1}}=H_{14}(s,\psi)+H_{15}(s,\psi),\quad\text{say}.

We can write

H_{1}(s,\psi)H_{2}(s,\psi)=B(s,\psi)+H_{15}(s,\psi)H_{2}(s,\psi)

with

[formula]

B(s,\psi)=(H_{14}(s,\psi)+\iota_{2}H_{12}(s,\psi))H_{2}(s,\psi).

Accordingly we have

[formula]

\Xi_{13}=\Xi_{14}+\Xi_{15}

with

[formula]

\Xi_{14}=\sum_{\psi\in\Psi_{1}}\,\sum_{\rho\in\tilde{Z}(\psi)}\mathcal{C}^{*}(\rho,\psi)Z(\rho,\chi\psi)^{-1}B(\rho,\psi)\omega(\rho),

[formula]

\Xi_{15}=\sum_{\psi\in\Psi_{1}}\,\sum_{\rho\in\tilde{Z}(\psi)}\mathcal{C}^{*}(\rho,\psi)Z(\rho,\chi\psi)^{-1}H_{15}(\rho,\psi)H_{2}(\rho,\psi)\omega(\rho).

The goal of this section is to evaluate
\Xi_{15}
.

Write

\varkappa_{12}(y)=\frac{1}{0.504}\bigg(\frac{P_{1}}{y}\bigg)^{\beta_{6}}\int_{0.5}^{0.504}\bigg\{g\bigg(\frac{P^{z}}{y}\bigg)-g\bigg(\frac{P^{0.5}}{y}\bigg)\bigg\}\,dz

and

\tilde{H}_{15}(s,\psi)=\sum_{P^{0.5}\eta_{-}<n<P_{1}\eta_{+}}\frac{\varkappa_{12}(n)\chi\psi(n)}{n^{s}}.

We first claim that

[formula]

\sum_{\psi\in\Psi_{1}}\,\sum_{\rho\in\tilde{Z}(\psi)}\mathcal{C}^{*}(\rho,\psi)|H_{15}(\rho,\psi)-\tilde{H}_{15}(\rho,\psi)|^{2}\omega(\rho)=o(\mathfrak{a}\mathcal{P}).

By the argument in the last section, for
P^{0.5}\eta_{+}<n<P_{1}\eta_{-}
,

\int_{0.5}^{0.504}g\bigg(\frac{P^{z}}{y}\bigg)\,dz=\frac{\log P_{1}-\log n}{\log P}+O(\varepsilon),

so that

\varkappa_{12}(n)-\varkappa_{1}(n)\ll\varepsilon.

Also we have

\varkappa_{12}(n)-\varkappa_{1}(n)\ll\begin{cases}\mathcal{L}^{-10}&\quad\text{if}\quad P_{1}\eta_{-}\leq n<P_{1}\eta_{+},\\
1&\quad\text{if}\quad P^{0.5}\eta_{-}<n\leq P^{0.5}\eta_{+}.\end{cases}

These bounds together with (8.25) and (8.26) imply (12.6). We briefly describe the argument as follows. Let
\mathfrak{t}(y)

denote the characteristic function of the interval
(P^{0.5}\eta_{-},P^{0.5}\eta_{+}]
. If
d\leq P^{0.5}(\eta_{+}-\eta_{-})
, then

\sum_{l}\frac{\mathfrak{t}(dl)}{l}\ll\mathcal{L}^{-10}.

If
d>P^{0.5}(\eta_{+}-\eta_{-})
, then there exists at most one
l
such that
\mathfrak{t}(dl)=1
. Hence

\sum_{d>P^{0.5}(\eta_{+}-\eta_{-})}\frac{1}{d}\bigg(\sum_{l}\frac{\mathfrak{t}(dl)}{l}\bigg)\leq\sum_{l}\frac{1}{l^{2}}\sum_{d}\frac{\mathfrak{t}(dl)}{d}\ll\mathcal{L}^{-10}.

Assume
\sigma=1/2
,
|t-2\pi t_{0}|<\mathcal{L}_{1}
and
0.5\leq z\leq 0.504
. Using the proof of Lemma 11.2 with
s+\beta_{6}
in place of
s
we deduce that

\displaystyle\tilde{H}_{15}(s,\psi)=

\displaystyle\frac{Z(s+\beta_{6},\chi\psi)P_{1}^{\beta_{6}}}{0.504}\sum_{n}\frac{\chi\bar{\psi}(n)}{n^{1-s-\beta_{6}}}\int_{0.496}^{0.5}\bigg\{g\bigg(\frac{P^{0.5}Dt_{0}}{n}\bigg)-g\bigg(\frac{P^{z}Dt_{0}}{n}\bigg)\bigg\}\,dz

\displaystyle+O(E(s+\beta_{6},\psi)).

Write

P^{\prime\prime}_{1}=P^{0.496}Dt_{0},\qquad P^{\prime\prime}_{2}=P^{0.5}Dt_{0}.

By (4) and (4), the terms with
n\leq P^{\prime\prime}_{1}\eta_{-}
or
n\geq P^{\prime\prime}_{2}\eta_{+}
above contribute
\ll\varepsilon
. If

P^{\prime\prime}_{1}\eta_{+}<n<P^{\prime\prime}_{2}\eta_{-},

then, in a way similar to the proof of (10.),

\int_{0.496}^{0.5}\bigg\{g\bigg(\frac{P^{0.5}Dt_{0}}{n}\bigg)-g\bigg(\frac{P^{z}Dt_{0}}{n}\bigg)\bigg\}\,dz=\frac{\log n}{\log P}-\tilde{\alpha}-0.496+O(\varepsilon).

Thus, in a way similar to the proof of Proposition 2.6, by the above discussion we deduce that

[formula]

\sum_{\psi\in\Psi_{1}}\,\sum_{\rho\in\tilde{Z}(\psi)}\mathcal{C}^{*}(\rho,\psi)|Z(\rho,\chi\psi)^{-1}\tilde{H}_{15}(\rho,\psi)-\bar{H}_{16}(1-s,\bar{\psi})|^{2}\omega(\rho)=o(\mathfrak{a}\mathcal{P}).

where

\bar{H}_{16}(1-s,\bar{\psi})=\frac{1}{\log P_{1}}\sum_{P^{\prime\prime}_{1}<n<P^{\prime\prime}_{2}}\frac{\chi\bar{\psi}(n)}{n^{1-s}}\big(n/P^{\prime\prime}_{1}\big)^{\beta_{6}}\log\big(n/P^{\prime\prime}_{1}\big).

By (12.7) and (12.8) we obtain

\Xi_{15}=\sum_{\psi\in\Psi_{1}}\,\sum_{\rho\in\tilde{Z}(\psi)}\mathcal{C}^{*}(\rho,\psi)\bar{H}_{16}(1-\rho,\bar{\psi})H_{2}(\rho,\psi)\omega(\rho)+o(\mathcal{P}).

This implies, by Lemma 8.1,

[formula]

\Xi_{15}=\Theta_{1}(\mathbb{a}_{12},\mathbb{a}_{25})+\overline{\Theta_{1}(\mathbb{a}_{15},\mathbb{a}_{22})}+o(\mathcal{P})

where

a_{15}(n)=\chi(n)\varkappa_{13}(n)

with

\varkappa_{13}(n)=\begin{cases}(\log P_{1})^{-1}(n/P^{\prime\prime}_{1})^{-\beta_{6}}\log(n/P^{\prime\prime}_{1})&\quad\text{if}\quad P^{\prime\prime}_{1}<n<P^{\prime\prime}_{2},\\
0&\quad\text{otherwise},\end{cases}

a_{25}(n)=\overline{a_{15}(n)}.

Assume
1\leq j\leq 3
in what follows.

Lemma 12.1.

We have

\sum_{l}\frac{\chi(l)\varkappa_{13}(dl)}{l^{1-\beta_{j}}}\ll\begin{cases}T^{-c}&\quad\text{if}\quad d\leq P^{\prime\prime}_{1}/T,\\
\alpha_{1}&\quad\text{if}\quad P^{\prime\prime}_{1}/T<d\leq P^{\prime\prime}_{1},\end{cases}

and

\sum_{l}\frac{\chi(l)\varkappa_{13}(dl)}{l^{1-\beta_{j}}}=\frac{L^{\prime}(1,\chi)}{\log P_{1}}\big(-1+(2\beta_{6}-\beta_{j})\log(d/P^{\prime\prime}_{1})+\epsilon_{1j}(d)\big),\quad|\epsilon_{1j}(d)|<10^{-5},

if

P^{\prime\prime}_{1}<d<P_{2}
,.

Proof.
Note that
\varkappa_{13}(n)\ll\alpha_{1}
if
P^{\prime\prime}_{1}/T\leq n\leq P^{\prime\prime}_{1}T
. In the case
d\leq P^{\prime\prime}_{1}
the results follow by the Polya-Vinogradov inequality and partial summation. On the other hand, in the case
P^{\prime\prime}_{1}<d<P_{2}
the sum becomes

\displaystyle\frac{(d/P^{\prime\prime}_{1})^{-\beta_{6}}\log(d/P^{\prime\prime}_{1})}{\log P_{1}}

\displaystyle\sum_{l<P^{\prime\prime}_{2}/d}\frac{\chi(l)}{l^{1+\beta_{6}-\beta_{j}}}+\frac{(d/P^{\prime\prime}_{1})^{-\beta_{6}}}{\log P_{1}}\sum_{l<P^{\prime\prime}_{2}/d}\frac{\chi(l)\log l}{l^{1+\beta_{6}-\beta_{j}}}+O(T^{-c})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{L^{\prime}(1,\chi)(d/P^{\prime\prime}_{1})^{-\beta_{6}}}{\log P_{1}}\big(-1+(\beta_{6}-\beta_{j})\log(d/P^{\prime\prime}_{1})\big)+O(\mathcal{L}^{-15}).

Since
P^{\prime\prime}_{2}/d>T^{10}
and
(d/P^{\prime\prime}_{1})^{-\beta_{6}}=1-\beta_{6}\log(d/P^{\prime\prime}_{1})+...
,
the result follows by Lemma 5.8 and a simple estimates.
\Box

Lemma 12.2.

If
dr<P^{\prime\prime}_{1}/T
, then

[formula]

\sum_{l}\frac{\chi(l)\bar{\varkappa}_{13}(drl)\xi_{j}(l;d,r)}{l}=b^{*}L^{\prime}(1,\chi)\Pi(d,r)(\log P)\beta_{j+1}\beta_{j+2}+O

with

b^{*}=\frac{1}{0.504}\int_{0}^{0.004}ze^{3\pi iz/2}\,dz.

If
P^{\prime\prime}_{1}/T\leq n\leq P^{\prime\prime}_{1}
, then

[formula]

\sum_{l}\frac{\chi(l)\bar{\varkappa}_{13}(drl)\xi_{j}(l;d,r)}{l}\ll\alpha_{1};

Proof.
If
P^{\prime\prime}_{1}<n<P^{\prime\prime}_{2}
, then

\bar{\varkappa}_{13}(n)=-\frac{1}{\log P_{1}}\frac{\partial}{\partial w}(n/P^{\prime\prime}_{1})^{\beta_{6}-w}|_{w=0}.

Hence

\sum_{l}\frac{\chi(l)\bar{\varkappa}_{13}(drl)\xi_{j}(l;d,r)}{l}=-\frac{1}{\log P_{1}}\frac{\partial}{\partial w}\bigg((dr/P^{\prime\prime}_{1})^{\beta_{6}-w}\sum_{P^{\prime\prime}_{1}/dr<l<P^{\prime\prime}_{2}/dr}\frac{\chi(l)\xi_{j}(l;d,r)}{l^{1-\beta_{6}+w}}\bigg)|_{w=0}.

Suppose
dr\leq P^{\prime\prime}_{1}/T
. By (4) and (4), for
|w|=\alpha
we have

\displaystyle\sum_{P^{\prime\prime}_{1}/dr<l<P^{\prime\prime}_{2}/dr}

\displaystyle\frac{\chi(l)\xi_{j}(l;d,r)}{l^{1-\beta_{6}+w}}=\sum_{l}\frac{\chi(l)\xi_{j}(l;d,r)}{l^{1-\beta_{6}+w}}\bigg\{g\bigg(\frac{P^{\prime\prime}_{2}}{drl}\bigg)-g\bigg(\frac{P^{\prime\prime}_{1}}{drl}\bigg)\bigg\}+O(\mathcal{L}^{-15})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2\pi i}\int_{(1)}\bigg(\sum_{l}\frac{\chi(l)\xi_{j}(l;d,r)}{l^{1-\beta_{6}+w+s}}\bigg)\big((P^{\prime\prime}_{2}/dr)^{s}-(P^{\prime\prime}_{1}/dr)^{s}\big)\frac{\omega_{1}(s)\,ds}{s}+O(\mathcal{L}^{-15}).

In a way similar to the proof of Lemma 8.4, by lemma 8.2 and 5.8, we find that the right side above is equal to

\displaystyle L^{\prime}(1,\chi)\Pi(d,r)

\displaystyle\cdot\frac{1}{2\pi i}\int_{|s|=5\alpha}\frac{(s+w-\beta_{6}+\beta_{j+1})(s+w-\beta_{6}+\beta_{j+2})}{s+w-\beta_{6}}\,\frac{(P^{\prime\prime}_{2}/dr)^{s}-(P^{\prime\prime}_{1}/dr)^{s}}{s}\,ds

\displaystyle+O(\mathcal{L}^{-15})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle L^{\prime}(1,\chi)\Pi(d,r)\beta_{j+1}\beta_{j+2}\frac{(P^{\prime\prime}_{2}/dr)^{\beta_{6}-w}-(P^{\prime\prime}_{1}/dr)^{\beta_{6}-w}}{\beta_{6}-w}+O(\mathcal{L}^{-15}).

It follows by Cauchy’ integral formula that

\frac{\partial}{\partial w}\bigg((dr/P^{\prime\prime}_{1})^{\beta_{6}-w}\sum_{P^{\prime\prime}_{1}/dr<l<P^{\prime\prime}_{2}/dr}\frac{\chi(l)\xi_{j}(l;d,r)}{l^{1-\beta_{6}+w}}\bigg)|_{w=0}=\frac{\partial}{\partial w}\bigg(\int_{1}^{P^{0.004}}y^{\beta_{6}-w-1}\,dy\bigg)|_{w=0}+O(\mathcal{L}^{-6})

The main term on the right side is, by substituting
y=P^{z}
, equal to

-(\log P)^{2}\int_{0}^{0.004}ze^{3\pi iz/2}\,dz.

Gathering these results together we obtain (12.10). The proof of (12.11) is similar to that of .

Lemma 12.3.

If
P^{\prime\prime}_{1}<dr<P_{2}
, then

\displaystyle\sum_{l}\frac{\chi(l)\bar{\varkappa}_{13}(drl)\xi_{j}(l;d,r)}{l}=

\displaystyle\frac{L^{\prime}(1,\chi)\Pi(d,r)}{\log P_{1}}\big(-1+(-2\beta_{6}+\beta_{j+1}+\beta_{j+2})\log(dr/P^{\prime\prime}_{1})+\epsilon_{2j}(dr)\big),

\displaystyle|\epsilon_{2j}(dr)|<10^{-5}.

Proof.
The left side is equal to

-\frac{1}{\log P_{1}}\frac{\partial}{\partial w}\bigg((dr/P^{\prime\prime}_{1})^{\beta_{6}-w}\sum_{l<P^{\prime\prime}_{2}/dr}\frac{\chi(l)\xi_{j}(l;d,r)}{l^{1-\beta_{6}+w}}\bigg)|_{w=0}.

Assume
|w|=\alpha
. In a way similar to the proof of Lemma 12.1, we deduce that

\displaystyle\sum_{l<P^{\prime\prime}_{2}/dr}

\displaystyle\frac{\chi(l)\xi_{j}(l;d,r)}{l^{1-\beta_{6}+w}}=L^{\prime}(1,\chi)\Pi(d,r)

\displaystyle\times\cdot\frac{1}{2\pi i}\int_{|s|=5\alpha}\frac{(s+w-\beta_{6}+\beta_{j+1})(s+w-\beta_{6}+\beta_{j+2})}{s+w-\beta_{6}}\,\frac{(P^{\prime\prime}_{2}/dr)^{s}}{s}\,ds+O(\mathcal{L}^{-15}).

By direct calculation,

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2\pi i}\int_{|s|=5\alpha}

\displaystyle\frac{(s+w-\beta_{6}+\beta_{j+1})(s+w-\beta_{6}+\beta_{j+2})}{s+w-\beta_{6}}\,\frac{(P^{\prime\prime}_{2}/dr)^{s}}{s}\,ds

\displaystyle=w-\beta_{6}+\beta_{j+1}+\beta_{j+2}+\beta_{j+1}\beta_{j+2}\int_{1}^{P^{\prime\prime}/dr}y^{\beta_{6}-w-1}\,dy,

and the derivative of

(dr/P^{\prime\prime}_{1})^{\beta_{6}-w}\bigg(w-\beta_{6}+\beta_{j+1}+\beta_{j+2}+\beta_{j+1}\beta_{j+2}\int_{1}^{P_{2}^{\prime\prime}/dr}y^{\beta_{6}-w-1}\,dy\bigg)

at
w=0
is equal to

\displaystyle(dr/P^{\prime\prime}_{1})^{\beta_{6}}

\displaystyle\bigg(1-\beta_{j+1}\beta_{j+2}\int_{1}^{P_{2}^{\prime\prime}/dr}y^{\beta_{6}-1}\log y\,dy\bigg)

\displaystyle-(dr/P^{\prime\prime}_{1})^{\beta_{6}}\log(dr/P^{\prime\prime}_{1})\bigg(-\beta_{6}+\beta_{j+1}+\beta_{j+2}+\beta_{j+1}\beta_{j+2}\int_{1}^{P_{2}^{\prime\prime}/dr}y^{\beta_{6}-1}\,dy\bigg).

This can be written as the form

1-(-2\beta_{6}+\beta_{j+1}+\beta_{j+2})\log(dr/P^{\prime\prime}_{1})-\epsilon_{2j}(dr),

since
(dr/P^{\prime\prime}_{1})^{\beta_{6}}=1+\beta_{6}\log(dr/P^{\prime\prime}_{1})+...
.
\Box

Evaluation of
\Theta_{1}(\mathbb{a}_{12},\mathbb{a}_{25})
.

We have

\displaystyle S_{j}(\mathbb{a}_{12},\mathbb{a}_{25})=

\displaystyle\sum_{r}\,\sum_{d}\frac{|\chi(d)||\mu\chi(r)|\lambda_{0j}(dr)}{dr\varphi(r)}\bigg(\sum_{m}\frac{\chi(m)\big(\bar{\iota}_{3}\varkappa_{3}(drm)+\bar{\iota}_{4}\varkappa_{2}(drm)\big)}{m^{1-\beta_{j}}}\bigg)

\displaystyle\times\bigg(\sum_{n}\frac{\chi(n)\bar{\varkappa}_{13}(n)\xi_{0j}(n;d,r)}{n}\bigg).

The sum is split into three sums according to
dr\leq P_{1}^{\prime\prime}/T
,
P_{1}^{\prime\prime}/T<dr\leq P^{\prime\prime}_{1}
and
P_{1}^{\prime\prime}<dr<P_{2}
. By lemma 8.2, 8.3 and 12.1,the sum over
P_{1}^{\prime\prime}/T<dr\leq P^{\prime\prime}_{1}
contributes
o(\alpha)
; the sum over
dr\leq P_{1}^{\prime\prime}/T
is equal to

[formula]

\displaystyle L^{\prime}(1,\chi)^{2}

\displaystyle b^{*}\beta_{j+1}\beta_{j+2}\sum_{n<P^{0.496}}\frac{|\chi(n)|\lambda_{0j}(n)}{\varphi(n)}\bigg(\frac{\bar{\iota}_{3}\mathcal{F}_{j6}(P^{0.498}/n)}{0.498}+\frac{\bar{\iota}_{4}\mathcal{F}_{j7}(P^{0.5}/n)}{0.5}\bigg)+o(\alpha)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathfrak{a}b^{*}(\log P)\beta_{j+1}\beta_{j+2}\int_{0}^{0.496}\bigg(\frac{\bar{\iota}_{3}\mathfrak{f}_{j6}(0.498-z)}{0.498}+\frac{\bar{\iota}_{4}\mathfrak{f}_{j7}(0.5-z)}{0.5}\bigg)\,dz+o(\alpha).

By lemma 8.2, 8.3 and 12.3, the sum over
P_{1}^{\prime\prime}<dr<P_{2}
is equal to

[formula]

\displaystyle\frac{L^{\prime}(1,\chi)^{2}\bar{\iota}_{3}}{(0.504)(0.498)\log^{2}P}\sum_{P^{0.496}<n<P^{0.498}}\frac{|\chi(n)|\lambda_{0j}(n)}{\varphi(n)}\mathcal{F}_{j6}(P^{0.498}/n)\mathcal{W}_{j}(n)

\displaystyle+\frac{L^{\prime}(1,\chi)^{2}\bar{\iota}_{4}}{(0.504)(0.5)\log^{2}P}\sum_{P^{0.496}<n<P^{0.5}}\frac{|\chi(n)|\lambda_{0j}(n)}{\varphi(n)}\mathcal{F}_{j7}(P^{0.5}/n)\mathcal{W}_{j}(n)+o(\alpha)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{\mathfrak{a}\bar{\iota}_{3}}{(0.504)(0.498)\log P}\int_{0.496}^{0.498}\mathfrak{f}_{j6}(0.498-z)\mathcal{W}_{j}(P^{z})\,dz

\displaystyle+\frac{\mathfrak{a}\bar{\iota}_{4}}{(0.504)(0.5)\log P}\int_{0.496}^{0.5}\mathfrak{f}_{j7}(0.5-z)\mathcal{W}_{j}(P^{z})\,dz+o(\alpha).

where

\mathcal{W}_{j}(n)=-1+(-2\beta_{6}+\beta_{j+1}+\beta_{j+2})\log(n/P^{\prime\prime}_{1})+\epsilon_{2j}(n).

For
0\leq z\leq 0.002
, a good approximation to
\mathfrak{f}_{j6}(z)
is

1+\pi i(3-j)z\simeq 1+(2\beta_{6}-\beta_{j})(\log P)z.

Since

4\beta_{6}-\beta_{1}-\beta_{2}-\beta_{3}\simeq 0,

it follows by simple calculation that

\int_{0.496}^{0.498}\mathfrak{f}_{j6}(0.498-z)\mathcal{W}_{j}(P^{z})\,dz=-0.002+\epsilon/10.

For
0\leq z\leq 0.004
, a good approximation to
\mathfrak{f}_{j7}(z)
is

1+\pi i(5-j)z\simeq 1+(2\beta_{7}-\beta_{j})(\log P)z.

Thus, for
0.496\leq z\leq 0.5
, the function
\mathfrak{f}_{j7}(0.5-z)\mathcal{W}_{j}(P^{z})
can be well approximated by

-1-(2\beta_{7}-\beta_{j})(\log P)(0.5-z)-(2\beta_{6}-\beta_{j+1}-\beta_{j+2})(\log P)(z-0.496).

Since

(2\beta_{6}+2\beta_{7}-\beta_{1}-\beta_{2}-\beta_{3})\log P\simeq 2\pi i,

it follows that

\int_{0.496}^{0.5}\mathfrak{f}_{j6}(0.5-z)\mathcal{W}_{j}(P^{z})\,dz=-0.004-\frac{\pi i}{250^{2}}+\epsilon/10.

inserting these results into (12.13), we find that the sum over
P_{1}^{\prime\prime}<dr<P_{2}
is equal to

[formula]

\frac{\mathfrak{a}}{0.504\log P}\bigg(-\frac{0.002\bar{\iota}_{3}}{0.498}-0.008\bar{\iota}_{4}-\frac{2\pi i\bar{\iota}_{4}}{250^{2}}+\epsilon/4\bigg).

It follows from (12.12) and (12.14) that

[formula]

\frac{1}{2\alpha}S_{1}(\mathbb{a}_{12},\mathbb{a}_{25})+\frac{2}{\alpha}S_{2}(\mathbb{a}_{12},\mathbb{a}_{25})+\frac{3}{2\alpha}S_{3}(\mathbb{a}_{12},\mathbb{a}_{25})=\mathfrak{a}(e_{1}^{*}+e_{2}^{*}+\epsilon/2)

where

e_{1}^{*}=-\pi b^{*}(3e_{11}^{*}+6e_{12}^{*}+3e_{13}^{*})

with

e_{1j}^{*}=\int_{0}^{0.496}\bigg(\frac{\bar{\iota}_{3}\mathfrak{f}_{j6}(0.498-z)}{0.498}+\frac{\bar{\iota}_{4}\mathfrak{f}_{j7}(0.5-z)}{0.5}\bigg)\,dz,

and

e_{2}^{*}=\frac{4}{0.504\pi}\bigg(-\frac{0.002\bar{\iota}_{3}}{0.498}-0.008\bar{\iota}_{4}-\frac{2\pi i\bar{\iota}_{4}}{250^{2}}\bigg).

Evaluation of
\Theta_{1}(\mathbb{a}_{15},\mathbb{a}_{22})
.

We have

\displaystyle S_{j}(\mathbb{a}_{15},\mathbb{a}_{22})=

\displaystyle\sum_{r}\,\sum_{d}\frac{|\chi(d)||\mu\chi(r)|\lambda_{0j}(dr)}{dr\varphi(r)}\bigg(\sum_{m}\frac{\chi(m)\varkappa_{13}(m)}{m^{1-\beta_{j}}}\bigg)

\displaystyle\times\bigg(\sum_{n}\frac{\chi(n)\big(\iota_{3}\bar{\varkappa}_{3}(drn)+\iota_{4}\bar{\varkappa}_{2}(drn)\big)\xi_{0j}(n;d,r)}{n}\bigg).

The sum is split into two sums according to
dr\leq P^{\prime\prime}_{1}
and
P_{1}^{\prime\prime}<dr<P_{2}
. By lemma 8.2, 8.3 and 12.1, the first sum contributes
o(\alpha)
; the second sum is equal to

\mathfrak{a}b^{*}\int_{0}^{0.496}\big(\bar{\iota}_{3}\mathfrak{f}_{j}6(0.498-z)+\bar{\iota}_{4}\mathfrak{f}_{j}6(0.5-z)\big)\,dz+o(\alpha).

By lemma 8.2, 8.3 and 12.3, the sum over
P_{1}^{\prime\prime}<dr<P_{2}
is equal to

\displaystyle\frac{L^{\prime}(1,\chi)^{2}\iota_{3}}{(0.504)(0.498)\log^{2}P}\sum_{P^{0.496}<n<P^{0.498}}\frac{|\chi(n)|\lambda_{0j}(n)}{\varphi(n)}\mathcal{G}_{j6}(P^{0.498}/n)\mathcal{W}^{*}_{j}(n)

\displaystyle+\frac{L^{\prime}(1,\chi)^{2}\iota_{4}}{(0.504)(0.5)\log^{2}P}\sum_{P^{0.496}<n<P^{0.5}}\frac{|\chi(n)|\lambda_{0j}(n)}{\varphi(n)}\mathcal{G}_{j7}(P^{0.5}/n)\mathcal{W}^{*}_{j}(n)+o(\alpha)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{\mathfrak{a}\iota_{3}}{(0.504)(0.498)\log P}\int_{0.496}^{0.498}\mathfrak{g}_{j6}(0.498-z)\mathcal{W}^{*}_{j}(P^{z})\,dz

\displaystyle+\frac{\mathfrak{a}\iota_{4}}{(0.504)(0.5)\log P}\int_{0.496}^{0.5}\mathfrak{g}_{j7}(0.5-z)\mathcal{W}^{*}_{j}(P^{z})\,dz+o(\alpha).

where

\mathcal{W}^{*}(n)=-1+(2\beta_{6}-\beta_{j})\log(n/P^{\prime\prime}_{1})+\epsilon_{1j}(n).

For
0\leq z\leq 0.004
and
\mu=6,7
, the function
\mathfrak{g}_{j\mu}(z)
is well-approximated by

1-(2\beta_{\mu}-\beta_{j+1}-\beta_{j+2})(\log P)z.

Thus, in a way similar to the evaluation of
\Theta_{1}(\mathbb{a}_{12},\mathbb{a}_{25})
, we deduce that

[formula]

\frac{1}{2\alpha}S_{1}(\mathbb{a}_{15},\mathbb{a}_{23})+\frac{2}{\alpha}S_{2}(\mathbb{a}_{15},\mathbb{a}_{22})+\frac{3}{2\alpha}S_{3}(\mathbb{a}_{15},\mathbb{a}_{22})=\mathfrak{a}(\overline{e_{2}^{*}}+\epsilon/2)

Finally, by (12.9), (12.15) and (12.16) we conclude

[formula]

\Xi_{15}=(e_{1}^{*}+2e_{2}^{*}+\epsilon)\mathfrak{a}\mathcal{P}.

13. Approximation to
\Xi_{14}

In this section we establish an approximation to
\Xi_{14}
.

Assume that
\psi\in\Psi_{1}
and
\rho\in\mathcal{Z}(\psi)
. By Lemma 5.2 and (2.2),

[formula]

\displaystyle\mathcal{C}^{*}(\rho,\psi)

\displaystyle=-iZ(\rho+\beta_{3},\psi)^{-1}\frac{L(\rho+\beta_{1},\psi)L(\rho+\beta_{2},\psi)L(\rho+\beta_{3},\psi)}{L^{\prime}(\rho,\psi)}(1+O(1/t_{0}))

\displaystyle=-i\frac{L(\rho+\beta_{1},\psi)L(\rho+\beta_{2},\psi)L(1-\rho-\beta_{3},\bar{\psi})}{L^{\prime}(\rho,\psi)}(1+O(\mathcal{L}^{-123})).

By Lemma 6.1,

L(1-\rho-\beta_{3},\bar{\psi})=\overline{L(\rho+\beta_{3},\psi)}=K(1-\rho-\beta_{3},\bar{\psi})+Z(\rho+\beta_{3},\psi)^{-1}N(\rho+\beta_{3},\psi)+O(E_{1}(\rho+\beta_{3},\psi)),

and, by Lemma 5.1,

Z(\rho+\beta_{3},\psi)^{-1}=Z(\rho+\beta_{2},\psi)^{-1}(pt_{0})^{\beta_{1}}+O(\mathcal{L}^{-123}).

Hence

[formula]

\displaystyle L(\rho

\displaystyle+\beta_{2},\psi)L(1-\rho-\beta_{3},\bar{\psi})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle L(\rho+\beta_{2},\psi)K(1-\rho-\beta_{3},\bar{\psi})+(pt_{0})^{\beta_{1}}N(\rho+\beta_{3},\psi)L(1-\rho-\beta_{2},\bar{\psi})

\displaystyle+O(|L(\rho+\beta_{2},\psi)N(\rho+\beta_{3},\psi)\mathcal{L}^{-123})+O(|L(\rho+\beta_{2},\psi)|E_{1}(\rho+\beta_{3},\psi)).

By Lemma 6.1 and 5.1,

\displaystyle(pt_{0})^{\beta_{1}}L(1-\rho-\beta_{2},\bar{\psi})=

\displaystyle(pt_{0})^{\beta_{1}}K(1-\rho-\beta_{2},\bar{\psi})+(pt_{0})^{\beta_{3}}Z(\rho,\psi)^{-1}N(\rho+\beta_{2},\psi)

\displaystyle+O(N(\rho+\beta_{2},\psi)\mathcal{L}^{-123})+O(E_{1}(\rho+\beta_{2},\psi)).

We insert this into (13.2) and then insert the result into (13.1). Thus we obtain

[formula]

\displaystyle\mathcal{C}^{*}(\rho,\psi)=

\displaystyle-i\frac{L(\rho+\beta_{1},\psi)L(\rho+\beta_{2},\psi)}{L^{\prime}(\rho,\psi)}K(1-\rho-\beta_{3},\bar{\psi})

\displaystyle-i(pt_{0})^{\beta_{1}}\frac{L(\rho+\beta_{1},\psi)}{L^{\prime}(\rho,\psi)}N(\rho+\beta_{3},\psi)K(1-\rho-\beta_{2},\bar{\psi})

\displaystyle-i(pt_{0})^{\beta_{3}}Z(\rho,\psi)^{-1}\frac{L(\rho+\beta_{1},\psi)}{L^{\prime}(\rho,\psi)}N(\rho+\beta_{2},\psi)N(\rho+\beta_{3},\psi)

\displaystyle+O(E_{1}^{*}(\rho,\psi))

where

\displaystyle E_{1}^{*}(\rho,\psi)=

\displaystyle\bigg|\frac{L(\rho+\beta_{1},\psi)L(\rho+\beta_{2},\psi)}{L^{\prime}(\rho,\psi)}\bigg|\big(|L(\rho+\beta_{3},\psi)|t_{0}^{-1}+|N(\rho+\beta_{3},\psi)|\mathcal{L}^{-123}+E_{1}(\rho+\beta_{3},\psi)\big)

\displaystyle+\bigg|\frac{L(\rho+\beta_{1},\psi)N(\rho+\beta_{3},\psi)}{L^{\prime}(\rho,\psi)}\bigg|\big(|N(\rho+\beta_{2},\psi)|\mathcal{L}^{-123}+E_{1}(\rho+\beta_{2},\psi)\big).

Recall that
B(s,\psi)
is given by (12.2). Multiplying (13.3) by
Z(\rho,\chi\psi)^{-1}B(\rho,\psi)
and applying Lemma 4.8 we obtain

\mathcal{C}^{*}(\rho,\psi)Z(\rho,\chi\psi)^{-1}B(\rho,\psi)=-i\big(\mathcal{K}^{*}_{1}(\rho,\psi)+(pt_{0})^{\beta_{1}}\mathcal{K}^{*}_{2}(\rho,\psi)-(pt_{0})^{\beta_{3}}\mathcal{K}^{*}_{3}(\rho,\psi)\big)+O(E_{1}^{*}(\rho,\psi)|B(\rho,\psi)|)

where

[formula]

\mathcal{K}^{*}_{1}(\rho,\psi)=Z(\rho,\chi\psi)^{-1}\frac{L(\rho+\beta_{1},\psi)L(\rho+\beta_{2},\psi)}{L^{\prime}(\rho,\psi)}B(\rho,\psi)K(1-\rho-\beta_{3},\bar{\psi}),

[formula]

\mathcal{K}^{*}_{2}(\rho,\psi)=Z(\rho,\chi\psi)^{-1}\frac{L(\rho+\beta_{1},\psi)}{L^{\prime}(\rho,\psi)}B(\rho,\psi)N(\rho+\beta_{3},\psi)K(1-\rho-\beta_{2},\bar{\psi}),

[formula]

\mathcal{K}^{*}_{3}(\rho,\psi)=\frac{L(\rho+\beta_{1},\psi)}{L^{\prime}(\rho,\psi)}B(\rho,\psi)G(\rho,\psi)N(\rho+\beta_{2},\psi)N(\rho+\beta_{3},\psi)F(1-\rho,\bar{\psi}),

Inserting this into (12.4) we obtain

[formula]

\Xi_{14}=-i\big(\Phi_{1}+\Phi_{2}-\Phi_{3}\big)+O(\mathcal{E})

where

[formula]

\Phi_{1}=\sum_{\psi\in\Psi_{1}}\,\sum_{\rho\in\mathcal{Z}(\psi)}\mathcal{K}^{*}_{1}(\rho,\psi)\omega(\rho),

[formula]

\Phi_{2}=\sum_{\psi\in\Psi_{1}}(p_{\psi}t_{0})^{\beta_{1}}\,\sum_{\rho\in\mathcal{Z}(\psi)}\mathcal{K}^{*}_{2}(\rho,\psi)\omega(\rho),

[formula]

\Phi_{3}=\sum_{\psi\in\Psi_{1}}(p_{\psi}t_{0})^{\beta_{3}}\,\sum_{\rho\in\mathcal{Z}(\psi)}\mathcal{K}^{*}_{3}(\rho,\psi)\omega(\rho),

and

\mathcal{E}=\sum_{\psi\in\Psi_{1}}\,\sum_{\rho\in\mathcal{Z}(\psi)}E_{1}^{*}(\rho,\psi)|B(\rho,\psi)|\omega(\rho).

Combining (2.34), Cauchy’s inequality, Proposition 7.1, Lemma 5.9, 6.1 and 3.3, we can verify that

[formula]

\mathcal{E}=o(\mathcal{P}).

For example, by (2.34),

\displaystyle\sum_{\psi\in\Psi_{1}}

\displaystyle\sum_{\rho\in\mathcal{Z}(\psi)}\bigg|\frac{L(\rho+\beta_{1},\psi)}{L^{\prime}(\rho,\psi)}\bigg||L(\rho+\beta_{2},\psi)|^{2}\omega(\rho)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{2\pi}\sum_{\psi\in\Psi_{1}}\bigg(\int_{\mathcal{J}(\alpha)}-\int_{\mathcal{J}(-\alpha)}\bigg)\frac{M(s+\beta_{1},\psi)}{M(s,\psi)}L(s+\beta_{2},\psi)L(1-s-\beta_{2},\bar{\psi})\omega(s)\,ds+O(\varepsilon),

the right side being estimated via Lemma 5.9, 6.1 and 3.3.

14. Mean-value formula II

Recall that we always assume
\psi
is a primitive character
(\bmod\,p)
,
p\sim P
. Sometimes we write
p_{\psi}
for the modulus
p
.

Let
\mathbb{k}^{*}=\{\kappa^{*}(m)\}
and
\mathbb{a}^{*}=\{a^{*}(n)\}
denote sequences of complex numbers satisfying

[formula]

\kappa^{*}(m)\ll\tau_{5}(m),

[formula]

\quad a^{*}(n)\ll 1,\quad a^{*}(n)=0\quad\text{if}\quad n>2P_{4}.

For
\beta\in\mathbf{C}
write

\Theta_{2}(\beta,\mathbb{k}^{*},\mathbb{a}^{*})=\sum_{\psi\in\Psi_{1}}\frac{(p_{\psi}t_{0})^{\beta}}{2\pi i}\int_{\mathcal{J}(1)}Z(s,\chi\psi)^{-1}\bigg(\sum_{m}\frac{\kappa^{*}(m)\psi(m)}{m^{s}}\bigg)\bigg(\sum_{n}\frac{a^{*}(n)\bar{\psi}(n)}{n^{1-s}}\bigg)\omega(s)\,ds.

The goal of this section is to prove

Proposition 14.1.

Suppose
|\beta|<5\alpha
. Then

\Theta_{2}(\beta,\mathbb{k}^{*},\mathbb{a}^{*})=\frac{1}{\varphi(D)}\sum_{p\sim P}(pt_{0})^{\beta}\,\sum_{d}\frac{1}{d}\,\sum_{k}\frac{\mu\chi(k)a^{*}(dk)}{k\varphi(k)}\sum_{(l,k)=1}\chi(l)\kappa^{*}(dl)\Delta\bigg(\frac{l}{Dpk}\bigg)+o(\mathcal{P}).

Proof.
We prove this proposition with
\beta=0
only, as the general case is almost identical. Write
\Theta_{2}
for
\Theta_{2}(0,\mathbb{k}^{*},\mathbb{a}^{*})
. Similar to the proof of Proposition 7.1, in the expression for
\Theta_{2}
, we can extend the sum over
\Psi_{1}
to the sum over
\Psi
, with acceptable errors. Namely we have

[formula]

\Theta_{2}=\sum_{\psi\in\Psi}\tilde{I}_{2}(\psi)+o(\mathcal{P})

where

\tilde{I}_{2}(\psi)=\frac{1}{2\pi i}\int_{\mathcal{J}(1)}Z(s,\chi\psi)^{-1}\bigg(\sum_{m}\frac{\kappa^{*}(m)\psi(m)}{m^{s}}\bigg)\bigg(\sum_{n}\frac{a^{*}(n)\bar{\psi}(n)}{n^{1-s}}\bigg)\omega(s)\,ds.

Assume
\psi(\bmod\,p)\in\Psi
. We use (2.5) with
\theta=\chi\psi
and then replace the segment
\mathcal{J}(1)
by the vertical line
\sigma=3/2
with a negligible error. Thus, by integration term by term,

\tilde{I}_{2}(\psi)=\frac{\tau(\chi\bar{\psi})}{Dp}\sum_{m}\,\sum_{n}\frac{\kappa^{*}(m)a^{*}(n)\psi(m)\bar{\psi}(n)}{n}\Delta_{1}\bigg(\frac{m}{Dpn}\bigg)+O(\varepsilon).

For
(mn,p)=1
we have

\sum_{\psi(\bmod\,p)}\tau(\chi\bar{\psi})\psi(m)\bar{\psi}(n)=\tau(\chi)\chi(p)(p-1)e\bigg(\frac{m\overline{Dn}}{p}\bigg).

Note that
(n,p)=1
if
n<2P_{4}
and
|\tau(\chi\psi_{p}^{0})|=\sqrt{D}
. Hence, substituting
d=(m,n)
,
m=dl
,
n=dk
and inverting the order of summation we obtain

\displaystyle\sideset{}{{}^{*}}{\sum}_{\psi(\bmod\,p)}\tilde{I}_{2}(\psi)=

\displaystyle\frac{\tau(\chi)\chi(p)}{D}\sum_{m}\,\sum_{n}\frac{\kappa^{*}(m)a^{*}(n)}{n}\Delta_{1}\bigg(\frac{m}{Dpn}\bigg)e\bigg(\frac{m\overline{Dn}}{p}\bigg)+O(PT^{-c})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{\tau(\chi)\chi(p)}{D}\sum_{d}\frac{1}{d}\,\sum_{k}\frac{a^{*}(dk)}{k}\sum_{(l,k)=1}\kappa^{*}(dl)\Delta_{1}\bigg(\frac{l}{Dpk}\bigg)e\bigg(\frac{l\overline{Dk}}{p}\bigg)+O(PT^{-c}).

(here we have replaced the constraint
(l,pk)=1
by
(l,k)=1
with an acceptable error, and used a bound for
|\Delta_{1}(x)|=|\Delta(x)|
given by Lemma 5.3). Since

\frac{\overline{Dk}}{p}\equiv-\frac{\bar{p}}{Dk}+\frac{1}{Dpk}\quad(\bmod\,1),

it follows that

\Delta_{1}\bigg(\frac{l}{Dpk}\bigg)e\bigg(\frac{l\overline{Dk}}{p}\bigg)=\Delta\bigg(\frac{l}{Dpk}\bigg)e\bigg(-\frac{l\bar{p}}{Dk}\bigg)

with
\bar{p}p\equiv 1(\bmod\,Dk)
. Hence

[formula]

\displaystyle\sideset{}{{}^{*}}{\sum}_{\psi(\bmod\,p)}\tilde{I}_{2}(\psi)=

\displaystyle\frac{\tau(\chi)\chi(p)}{D}\sum_{d}\frac{1}{d}\,\sum_{k}\frac{a^{*}(dk)}{k}\sum_{(l,k)=1}\kappa^{*}(dl)\Delta\bigg(\frac{l}{Dpk}\bigg)e\bigg(-\frac{l\bar{p}}{Dk}\bigg)

\displaystyle+O(PT^{-c}).

Assume
(k,p)=1
. If
(l,k)=1
and
(l,D)=D_{1}
, then
(k,D_{1})=1
. Thus, substituting
(l,D)=D_{1}
and
l=D_{1}l_{1}
, we find that
the innermost sum in (14.4) is equal to

\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}D=D_{1}D_{2}\\
(D_{1},k)=1\end{subarray}}\sum_{(l_{1},D_{2}k)=1}(\kappa_{1}*b)(D_{1}dl_{1})\Delta\bigg(\frac{l_{1}}{D_{2}pk}\bigg)e\bigg(-\frac{l_{1}\bar{p}}{D_{2}k}\bigg).

Inserting this into (14.4) and rearranging the terms we conclude

\displaystyle\sideset{}{{}^{*}}{\sum}_{\psi(\bmod\,p)}\tilde{I}_{2}(\psi)=\frac{\tau(\chi)\chi(p)}{D}\sum_{D=D_{1}D_{2}}\mathcal{S}(D_{1},D_{2};p)+O(PT^{-c}),

where

\mathcal{S}(D_{1},D_{2};p)=\sum_{d}\frac{1}{d}\sum_{(k,D_{1})=1}\frac{a^{*}(dk)}{k}\sum_{(l,D_{2}k)=1}\kappa^{*}(D_{1}dl)\Delta\bigg(\frac{l}{D_{2}pk}\bigg)e\bigg(-\frac{l\bar{p}}{D_{2}k}\bigg)

(here we have rewritten
l
for
l_{1}
).
Since
\chi(-1)\tau(\chi)^{2}=D
, the proof of Proposition 14.1 is now reduced to showing that

[formula]

\displaystyle\sum_{p\sim P}\chi(p)

\displaystyle\mathcal{S}(1,D;p)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{\chi(-1)\tau(\chi)}{\varphi(D)}\sum_{d}\frac{1}{d}\,\sum_{k}\frac{\mu\chi(k)a^{*}(dk)}{k\varphi(k)}\sum_{(l,k)=1}\chi(l)\kappa^{*}(dl)\Delta\bigg(\frac{l}{Dpk}\bigg)

\displaystyle+O(P^{2}D^{1/2-c})

and, for
D=D_{1}D_{2}
,
D_{1}>1
,

[formula]

\sum_{p\sim P}\chi(p)\mathcal{S}(D_{1},D_{2};p)\ll P^{2}D^{1/2-c}.

We first prove (14.5). Assume
k<2P_{4}
and
(l,Dk)=1
. Then

e\bigg(-\frac{l\bar{p}}{Dk}\bigg)=\frac{1}{\varphi(Dk)}\sum_{\theta(\bmod\,Dk)}\tau(\bar{\theta})\theta(-l)\bar{\theta}(p).

Hence

[formula]

\mathcal{S}(1,D;p)=\sum_{d}\frac{1}{d}\sum_{k}\frac{a^{*}(dk)}{k\varphi(Dk)}\sum_{\theta(\bmod\,Dk)}\tau(\bar{\theta})\bar{\theta}(p)\sum_{l}\kappa^{*}(dl)\theta(-l)\Delta\bigg(\frac{l}{Dpk}\bigg)

(here we have remove the constraint
(l,Dk)=1
as it is superfluous).

If
\theta
is the principal character
\psi_{Dk}^{0}
, then
\tau(\bar{\theta})=\mu(Dk)
. The total contribution from the
\theta=\psi_{Dk}^{0}
term to the right side above is, by Lemma 5.3,

\ll\sum_{d}\frac{1}{d}\sum_{k}\sum_{l}\frac{|(\kappa_{1}*b)(dl)a^{*}(dk)|}{\varphi(Dk)k}\bigg|\Delta\bigg(\frac{l}{Dpk}\bigg)\bigg|\ll P\mathcal{L}^{c}

which is admissible for (14.5). Now, let
\theta_{k}^{1}
be the character
(\bmod\,Dk)
induced by the primitive character
\chi(\bmod\,D)
which is real. Then

\tau(\theta_{k}^{1})\theta_{k}^{1}(p)\theta_{k}^{1}(-l)=\tau(\chi)\mu\chi(k)\chi(-pl).

Hence

\displaystyle\sum_{d}\frac{1}{d}

\displaystyle\sum_{k}\sum_{(l,Dk)=1}\frac{\kappa^{*}(dl)a^{*}(dk)}{\varphi(Dk)k}\Delta\bigg(\frac{l}{Dpk}\bigg)\tau(\theta_{k}^{1})\theta_{k}^{1}(p)\theta_{k}^{1}(-l)

\displaystyle=\frac{\chi(-p)\tau(\chi)}{\varphi(D)}\sum_{d}\frac{1}{d}\,\sum_{k}\frac{\mu\chi(k)a^{*}(dk)}{k\varphi(k)}\sum_{(l,k)=1}\chi(l)\kappa^{*}(dl)\Delta\bigg(\frac{l}{Dpk}\bigg).

By the above discussion, to complete the proof of (14.5), it now suffices to show that the total contribution from the terms with
\theta\neq\psi_{Dk}^{0}
and
\theta\neq\theta_{k}^{1}
in (14.7) to the left side of (14.5) is
O(P^{2}D^{-c})
. Namely we need to prove

[formula]

\displaystyle\sum_{d}\frac{1}{d}

\displaystyle\sum_{k}\frac{|a^{*}(dk)|}{\varphi(Dk)k}\sideset{}{{}^{\prime}}{\sum}_{\begin{subarray}{c}\theta(\bmod\,Dk)\\
\theta\neq\theta_{k}^{1}\end{subarray}}|\tau(\bar{\theta})|\bigg|\sum_{l}\kappa^{*}(dl)\theta(l)\sum_{p\sim P}\chi\bar{\theta}(p)\Delta\bigg(\frac{l}{Dpk}\bigg)\bigg|

\displaystyle\ll P^{2}D^{-c}.

If
\theta(\bmod\,Dk)
is induced by a primitive character
\theta^{*}(\bmod\,r)
, then
r|Dk
and
|\tau(\bar{\theta})|\leq\sqrt{r}
. For
r|Dk
we have

\frac{Dk}{r}\equiv 0\bigg(\bmod\,\frac{D}{(D,r)}\bigg).

Thus, substituting
Dk=hr
, we see that the left side of (14.8) is

\displaystyle\ll\sum_{d}\frac{1}{d}\,\sum_{1<r<2DP_{4}}\,\,\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}h<P/r\\
h\equiv 0(D/(D,r))\end{subarray}}\frac{D}{\varphi(hr)h\sqrt{r}}\sideset{}{{}^{*}}{\sum}_{\begin{subarray}{c}\theta(\bmod\,r)\\
\theta\neq\chi\end{subarray}}\bigg|\sum_{(l,h)=1}\kappa^{*}(dl)\theta(l)\sum_{p\sim P}\chi\bar{\theta}(p)\Delta\bigg(\frac{l}{phr}\bigg)\bigg|.

The range for
r
is divided into two parts according to
1<r<D^{3}
and
D^{3}\leq r<2DP_{4}
.
In a way similar to the proof of Proposition 7.1, for
1<r<D^{3}
we use the Mellin transform, Lemma 5.4 (i) and Lemma 5.6;
for
D^{3}\leq r<2DP_{4}
we use the Mellin transform, Lemma 5.4 (i) and the large sieve inequality. Thus we obtain (14.5).

The proof of (14.6) is analogous. In the case
D=D_{1}D_{2}
,
D_{1}>1
, the major difference from the proof of (14.5) is that the constraint
(k,D_{1})=1

implies
D\nmid D_{2}k
, so that any non-principal character
\theta(\bmod\,D_{2}k)
can not be induced by the real character
\chi(\bmod\,D)
. Thus, when the left side of (14.6) is treated in much the same way as (14.5), the main terms involved in the proof of (14.5) do not appear. This yields (14.6).
\Box

15. Evaluation of
\Phi_{1}

Recall that
\Phi_{1}
is given by (13.8). In view of (12.2),
B(s,\psi)
can be written as

[formula]

B(s,\psi)=\sum_{n}\frac{b(n)\chi\psi(n)}{n^{s}}

with

[formula]

b(n)\ll\tau_{2}(n),\qquad b(n)=0\quad\text{if}\quad n>PT^{-2}\eta_{+}.

Write

\mathcal{K}_{1}(s,\psi)=Z(s,\chi\psi)^{-1}\frac{L(s+\beta_{1},\psi)L(s+\beta_{2},\psi)}{L(s,\psi)}B(s,\psi)K(1-s-\beta_{3},\bar{\psi}).

Similar to (8.1), for
\psi\in\Psi_{1}
we have

\sum_{\rho\in\mathcal{Z}(\psi)}\mathcal{K}^{*}_{1}(\rho,\psi)\omega(\rho)=I_{2}^{+}(\psi)-I_{2}^{-}(\psi)+O(\varepsilon)

where

I_{2}^{\pm}(\psi)=\frac{1}{2\pi i}\int_{\mathcal{J}(\pm\alpha)}\mathcal{K}_{1}(s,\psi)\omega(s)\,ds.

Hence

[formula]

\Phi_{1}=\sum_{\psi\in\Psi_{1}}\big(I_{2}^{+}(\psi)-I_{2}^{-}(\psi)\big)+O(\varepsilon).

First we prove that

[formula]

\sum_{\psi\in\Psi_{1}}I_{2}^{-}(\psi)=o(\mathcal{P}).

Assume
\psi\in\Psi_{1}
and
s\in\mathcal{J}(-\alpha)
. By (2.2) and Lemma 5.1,

\frac{L(s+\beta_{1},\psi)L(s+\beta_{2},\psi)}{L(s,\psi)}=(pt_{0})^{-\beta_{3}}Z(s,\psi)\frac{L(1-s-\beta_{1},\bar{\psi})L(1-s-\beta_{2},\bar{\psi})}{L(1-s,\bar{\psi})}(1+O(\mathcal{L}^{-123})).

In a way similar to the proof of Lemma 8.1, it can be shown that the total contribution of the
O(\mathcal{L}^{-123})
term to the left side of (15.4) is
o(\mathcal{P})
. On the other hand, by (2.4), (2.5) and the relation

\tau(\chi\psi)=\tau(\chi)\tau(\psi)\psi(D)\chi(p),

we have

\frac{Z(s,\psi)}{Z(s,\chi\psi)}=\tau(\chi)\chi(p)\bar{\psi}(D)D^{s-1}(1+O(e^{-\pi t})).

For
\sigma<0
we can write

\frac{L(1-s-\beta_{1},\bar{\psi})L(1-s-\beta_{2},\bar{\psi})K(1-s-\beta_{3},\bar{\psi})}{L(1-s,\bar{\psi})}=\sum_{m}\frac{\tilde{k}(m)\bar{\psi}(m)}{m^{1-s}}

with
\tilde{k}(m)\ll\tau_{4}(m)
. Hence, moving the segment
\mathcal{J}(-\alpha)
to
\mathcal{J}(-1)
with a negligible error, we find that

\sum_{\psi\in\Psi_{1}}I_{2}^{-}(\psi)=\tau(\chi)\sum_{\psi\in\Psi_{1}}\chi(p_{\psi})(p_{\psi}t_{0})^{-\beta_{3}}\cdot\frac{1}{2\pi i}\int_{\mathcal{J}(-1)}\bigg(\sum_{m}\frac{\tilde{k}(m)\bar{\psi}(Dm)}{(Dm)^{1-s}}\bigg)B(s,\psi)\omega(s)\,ds+o(\mathcal{P}).

In a way similar to the proof of (7.3), the sum over
\psi\in\Psi_{1}
can be extended to the sum over
\psi\in\Psi
, and then the segment
\mathcal{J}(-1)
can be replaced by the line
\sigma=-1/2
, with acceptable errors. Thus the right side above is equal to

\tau(\chi)\sum_{p\sim P}\chi(p)(pt_{0})^{-\beta_{3}}\sideset{}{{}^{*}}{\sum}_{\psi(\bmod\,p)}\frac{1}{2\pi i}\int_{(-1/2)}\bigg(\sum_{m}\frac{\tilde{k}(m)\bar{\psi}(Dm)}{(Dm)^{1-s}}\bigg)B(s,\psi)\omega(s)\,ds+o(\mathcal{P}).

Since

\frac{1}{2\pi i}\int_{(-1/2)}\frac{\omega(s)\,ds}{n^{s}(Dm)^{1-s}}=\frac{1}{Dm}\bigg(\frac{Dm}{n}\bigg)^{s_{0}}\exp\bigg\{-\mathcal{L}_{2}^{2}\log^{2}\frac{Dm}{n}\bigg\}

and, for
n<PT^{-5}
and
Dm<2n
,

\sideset{}{{}^{*}}{\sum}_{\psi(\bmod\,p)}\psi(n)\bar{\psi}(Dm)\ll\begin{cases}p&\quad\text{if}\quad n=Dm,\\
1&\quad\text{if}\quad n\neq Dm,\end{cases}

it follows that

\sideset{}{{}^{*}}{\sum}_{\psi(\bmod\,p)}\frac{1}{2\pi i}\int_{(-1/2)}\bigg(\sum_{m}\frac{\tilde{k}(m)\bar{\psi}(Dm)}{(Dm)^{1-s}}\bigg)B(s,\psi)\omega(s)\,ds\ll PD^{-4/5}.

This yields (15.4).

Let
\kappa_{1}(m)
be given by

\frac{\zeta(s+\beta_{1})\zeta(s+\beta_{2})}{\zeta(s)}=\sum_{m}\frac{\kappa_{1}(m)}{m^{s}},\quad\sigma>1.

Regarding
b
as an arithmetic function, for
\sigma>1
we have

\frac{L(s+\beta_{1},\psi)L(s+\beta_{2},\psi)B(s,\psi)}{L(s,\psi)}=\sum_{m}\frac{(\kappa_{1}*b)(m)\psi(m)}{m^{s}}.

On the other hand, we can write

K(1-s-\beta_{3},\bar{\psi})=\sum_{n}\frac{\tilde{g}_{3}(n)\bar{\psi}(n)}{n^{1-s}}

with

\tilde{g}_{3}(y)=y^{\beta_{3}}g^{*}(P_{4}/y).

Hence, moving the segment
\mathcal{J}(\alpha)
to
\mathcal{J}(1)
with a negligible error, we obtain

[formula]

\sum_{\psi\in\Psi_{1}}I_{2}^{+}(\psi)=\Theta_{2}(0,\mathbb{k}^{*}_{1},\mathbb{a}_{1}^{*})+O(\varepsilon)

with

\kappa^{*}_{1}(m)=(\kappa_{1}*b)(m),\quad a^{*}_{1}(n)=\tilde{g}_{3}(n).

It follows by (15.3)-(15.5) and Proposition 14.1 that

[formula]

\Phi_{1}=\sum_{p\sim P}\Phi_{1}(p)+o(\mathcal{P})

where

[formula]

\Phi_{1}(p)=\frac{1}{\varphi(D)}\sum_{k}\frac{\mu\chi(k)}{k\varphi(k)}\sum_{d}\frac{\tilde{g}_{3}(dk)}{d}\,\sum_{(l,k)=1}(\kappa_{1}*b)(dl)\chi(l)\Delta\bigg(\frac{l}{Dpk}\bigg).

Assume
dk<2P_{4}
. Similar to (7.17),

(\kappa_{1}*b)(dl)=\sum_{d=d_{1}d_{2}}\,\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}l=l_{1}l_{2}\\
(l_{1},d_{2})=1\end{subarray}}b(d_{2}l_{2})\kappa_{1}(d_{1}l_{1}).

This yields

\displaystyle\sum_{(l,k)=1}(\kappa_{1}*b)(dl)\chi(l)\Delta\bigg(\frac{l}{Dpk}\bigg)=\sum_{d=d_{1}d_{2}}\,\sum_{(l_{2},k)=1}b(d_{2}l_{2})\chi(l_{2})\sum_{(l_{1},d_{2}k)=1}\kappa_{1}(d_{1}l_{1})\chi(l_{1})\Delta\bigg(\frac{l_{1}l_{2}}{Dpk}\bigg).

The innermost sum above is, by the Mellin transform, equal to

[formula]

\frac{1}{2\pi i}\int_{(2)}\bigg(\sum_{(l_{1},d_{2}k)=1}\frac{\kappa_{1}(d_{1}l_{1})\chi(l_{1})}{l_{1}^{s}}\bigg)\bigg(\frac{Dpk}{l_{2}}\bigg)^{s}\delta(s)\,ds.

Every
l_{1}
can be uniquely written as
l_{1}=hl
such that
h\in\mathcal{N}(d_{1})
and
(l,d_{1})=1
, so that
\kappa_{1}(d_{1}l_{1})=\kappa_{1}(d_{1}h)\kappa_{1}(l)
. Hence, for
\sigma>1
,

\displaystyle\sum_{(l_{1},d_{2}k)=1}\frac{\kappa_{1}(d_{1}l_{1})\chi(l_{1})}{l_{1}^{s}}=

\displaystyle\tilde{\kappa}_{1}(d_{1};d_{2}k,s)\sum_{(l,d_{1}d_{2}k)=1}\frac{\kappa_{1}(l)\chi(l)}{l^{s}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\tilde{\kappa}_{1}(d_{1};d_{2}k,s)\lambda_{1}(d_{1}d_{2}k,s)\frac{L(s+\beta_{1},\chi)L(s+\beta_{2},\chi)}{L(s,\chi)}

where

[formula]

\tilde{\kappa}_{1}(d_{1};r,s)=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}h\in\mathcal{N}(d_{1})\\
(h,r)=1\end{subarray}}\frac{\kappa_{1}(d_{1}h)\chi(h)}{h^{s}}

and

[formula]

\lambda_{1}(n,s)=\prod_{q|n}\frac{\big(1-\chi(q)q^{-s-\beta_{1}}\big)\big(1-\chi(q)q^{-s-\beta_{2}}\big)}{1-\chi(q)q^{-s}}.

For notational simplicity we shall write
\tilde{\kappa}_{1}(d;r)
and
\lambda_{1}(n)
for
\tilde{\kappa}_{1}(d;r,1)
and
\lambda_{1}(n,1)
respectively. Note that
Dpk/l_{2}>T
if
l_{2}<PT^{-2}
. In a way similar to the treatment of (7.19), by Lemma 5.5,
we see that the expression (15.8) is equal to the residue of the integrand at
s=\tilde{\rho}
plus an acceptable error. Further, by (5.15), in the expression for this residue, we can replace
\tilde{\rho}
by
1
with an acceptable error. Thus we have

\displaystyle\sum_{(l_{1},d_{2}k)=1}\kappa_{1}(d_{1}l_{1})\chi(l_{1})\Delta\bigg(\frac{l_{1}l_{2}}{Dpk}\bigg)=\mathcal{R}_{1}^{*}\bigg(\frac{Dpk}{l_{2}}\bigg)\tilde{\kappa}_{1}(d_{1};d_{2}k)\lambda_{1}(d_{1}d_{2}k)+O\bigg(\frac{\alpha^{100}\tau_{3}(d_{1})Dpk}{l_{2}}\bigg),

where

\mathcal{R}_{1}^{*}=\frac{L(1+\beta_{1},\chi)L(1+\beta_{2},\chi)}{L^{\prime}(1,\chi)}\delta(1).

This yields

\displaystyle\sum_{(l,k)=1}(\kappa_{1}*b)(dl)\chi(l)\Delta\bigg(\frac{l}{Dpk}\bigg)=

\displaystyle\mathcal{R}_{1}^{*}Dpk\sum_{d=d_{1}d_{2}}\tilde{\kappa}_{1}(d_{1};d_{2}k)\lambda_{1}(dk)\sum_{(l_{2},k)=1}\frac{b(d_{2}l_{2})\chi(l_{2})}{l_{2}}

\displaystyle+O\big(\alpha^{50}\tau_{3}(d_{1})Dpk\big).

Hence

\displaystyle\sum_{d}\frac{\tilde{g}_{3}(dk)}{d}

\displaystyle\sum_{(l,k)=1}(\kappa_{1}*b)(dl)\chi(l)\Delta\bigg(\frac{l}{Dpk}\bigg)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathcal{R}_{1}^{*}Dpk\sum_{d_{1}}\,\sum_{d_{2}}\frac{\tilde{g}_{3}(d_{1}d_{2}k)}{d_{1}d_{2}}\tilde{\kappa}_{1}(d_{1};d_{2}k)\lambda_{1}(d_{1}d_{2}k)\sum_{(l_{2},k)=1}\frac{b(d_{2}l_{2})\chi(l_{2})}{l_{2}}

\displaystyle+O\big(\alpha^{30}Dpk\big)

Inserting this into (15.7) and rewriting
d
,
l
and
m
for
d_{2}
,
l_{2}
and
d_{1}
respectively, we obtain

[formula]

\Phi_{1}(p)=\frac{\mathcal{R}_{1}^{*}Dp}{\varphi(D)}\sum_{d}\sum_{l}\frac{b(dl)\chi(l)}{dl}\mathcal{D}_{1}(d,l)+o(P)

where

\mathcal{D}_{1}(d,l)=\sum_{(k,l)=1}\frac{\mu\chi(k)}{\varphi(k)}\sum_{m}\frac{\tilde{\kappa}_{1}(m;dk)\lambda_{1}(mdk)\tilde{g}_{3}(mdk)}{m}.

On substituting
n=mk
we can write

[formula]

\mathcal{D}_{1}(d,l)=\sum_{n}\frac{\lambda_{1}(dn)\tilde{g}_{3}(dn)}{n}\xi_{1}(n;d,l)

with

[formula]

\xi_{1}(n;d,l)=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}n=mk\\
(k,l)=1\end{subarray}}\frac{\mu\chi(k)k}{\varphi(k)}\tilde{\kappa}_{1}(m;dk).

It can be verified, for given
d
and
l
, that
\xi_{1}(n;d,l)
is a multiplicative function of
n
. On the other hand, we have

\lambda_{1}(dn)=\lambda_{1}(d)\tilde{\lambda}_{1}(n,d)

with

\tilde{\lambda}_{1}(n,d)=\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}q|n\\
(q,d)=1\end{subarray}}\lambda_{1}(q).

Hence

\mathcal{D}_{1}(d,l)=\lambda_{1}(d)\sum_{n}\frac{\tilde{\lambda}_{1}(n,d)\xi_{1}(n;d,l)\tilde{g}_{3}(dn)}{n}.

On the right side above, we can replace the factor
\tilde{g}_{3}(dn)
by
(dn)^{\beta_{3}}g(P_{4}/(dn))
with a negligible error. Since

y^{\beta_{3}}g\bigg(\frac{P_{4}}{y}\bigg)=\frac{1}{2\pi i}\int_{(1)}\frac{P_{4}^{s+\beta_{3}}}{y^{s}}\frac{\omega_{1}(s+\beta_{3})}{s+\beta_{3}}\,ds,

it follows that

[formula]

\mathcal{D}_{1}(d,l)=\lambda_{1}(d)\cdot\frac{1}{2\pi i}\int_{(1)}\bigg(\sum_{n}\frac{\tilde{\lambda}_{1}(n,d)\xi_{1}(n;d,l)}{n^{1+s}}\bigg)\frac{P_{4}^{s+\beta_{3}}}{d^{s}}\,\frac{\omega_{1}(s+\beta_{3})}{s+\beta_{3}}\,ds+O(\varepsilon).

Note that
\tilde{\lambda}_{1}(q^{r},d)=\tilde{\lambda}_{1}(q,d)
for any
r
. If
\sigma>1
, then

\sum_{n}\frac{\tilde{\lambda}_{1}(n,d)\xi_{1}(n;d,l)}{n^{s}}=\prod_{q}\bigg(1+\tilde{\lambda}_{1}(q,d)\sum_{r}\frac{\xi_{1}(q^{r};d,l)}{q^{rs}}\bigg).

If
(q,dl)=1
, then

\xi_{1}(q;d,l)=\sum_{h\in\mathcal{N}(q)}\frac{\kappa_{1}(qh)\chi(h)}{h}-\frac{\chi(q)q}{q-1}=q^{-\beta_{1}}+q^{-\beta_{2}}-1-\chi(q)+O(1/q),

so that

\frac{(1-q^{-s-\beta_{1}})(1-q^{-s-\beta_{2}})}{(1-q^{-s})(1-\chi(q)q^{-s})}\bigg(1+\tilde{\lambda}_{1}(q,d)\sum_{r}\frac{\xi_{1}(q^{r};d,l)}{q^{rs}}\bigg)=1+O(q^{-19/10})

for
\sigma>9/10
. In case
(q,dl)>1
and
\sigma>9/10
, the left side above is trivially

1+O(q^{-9/10}).

It follows that the function
